  - `ov014_02205634`, written from case 10 of `02206050` (the sqrt block with `p.y + 0x1000`, `0x666` instead of `0x4cd`, and case 11 as case 1), is 6 / 7 by seqdiff in `d/v_b.cpp`. The hcorder climb and cxxperm get 3 / 3 (`perm/5634/out-006-*.cpp`, 8 in `match.py`). What is left is the `unk_84` store schedule: the target stores `x` right after loading `self->pos.x`, and stores `z` after the sqrt-register pool load. In the twin's target, which matches, the stores come in `y`, `z`, `x` order. Here, the copy-inline, struct-copy, pointer, `self->unk_84`-first and store-order variants all give the same three or four builds.
  - `ov000_0217a1f8` is the first part of `Lo_0217abd8` (stored at `0x44`, with `func_ov000_0217a328(self, 0x78, -1)` added). It is 3 diffs, the known `-1`-from-`0x78` derivation.
- A second cxxperm run of 20 minutes on each best (`--slack 2`, about 15,000–19,000 compiles) did not get either one lower (18 and 6).
- **Fragment search** (`scratchpad/twins1/t/frag.py mod func start end`) looks for the best window of matched code that is like an address range of a leftover. With registers masked, it only returned shared idioms (FX multiplies and vector copies). It did not help.
## Flag scope in a switch, BOOL returns as expressions, and fresh zeros (ov000 above 0x021a0000 and ov009–ov015, round ovhimid8)

- **Match: `func_ov010_02209948`** (new file `src/ov010/ov010_02209948.cpp`, 0x6e8 bytes, a wave/spawn state machine). The earlier draft was 19 / 5. Three changes matched it:
  - **Where the flag lives decides which register holds TRUE.** Case `0x10` uses a function-scope `BOOL ok;`, declared without an initialiser and set (`ok = TRUE;`) at the top of the case. Case `0x41` uses its own block-local `BOOL ok = TRUE;`. With one function-level `ok = TRUE` before the `switch`, the flag coalesced with the hoisted TRUE constant and every later register shifted.
  - **Write the `0x41` loop in place.** Do not put it in an `AllDone()` inline that returns the flag.
  - **Return a BOOL as an expression.** Write `return self->timer % (0x19 / (self->unk_0a - 1)) == 0;` and not `BOOL r = TRUE; if (...) r = FALSE; return r;`. The expression form builds the result after the division call (`moveq/movne`), as the target does.
- **A spilled local that the target reloads.** In `func_ov000_021b209c`, `actor = FindActor(id); sub = NULL; if (actor != NULL) sub = actor->unk_4c;` (an inline with an early `return NULL`) gives the target's `str r0, [sp, #0x1c]; ldr r0, [sp, #0x1c]` reload. That took the draft from 9 / 5 to 10 / 9 with one instruction left over (`ovhimid8/d/209c/k/A0B2.cpp`, match.py 501, because the pool shifts by one word). The target stores the hoisted zero directly (`streq r5, [sp, #0x1c]`). Our build first copies it into the call-result register (`moveq r0, r5; streq r0`). Five inline forms, a plain `if`/`else`, a ternary, `u8 *actor[1]` and `volatile` did not remove the copy.
- **Fresh zero against hoisted zero.** Some targets materialise a fresh `mov rX, #0` for one block and set the hoisted zero register only later; our build uses the hoisted register throughout, or the other way round. Seen in `ov015_0220b1b8`, `ov010_02201064` and `ov011_021fd638`. In `021fd638` our build hoists the `e->unk_08 = 0` zero to function entry, while the target hoists it only to the inner loop's preheader. Literal forms, inlines, zero locals and splitmod changed none of them. A 15-minute cxxperm on `021fd638` found nothing.
- **Constants are rematerialised at their use.** Compare `ov013_021fc7e0`: the target loads `0xb33` into `r2` while `0x800` is already in `r1` (the two overlap). None of the following made two constants overlap:
  - a constant passed through an inline parameter;
  - a local holding the constant;
  - a comma expression inside a call argument, e.g. `v14(0x800, (o->f3c = 0xb33, 0x800), 0x800)`.

  The same holds in `ov010_0220640c`, where the target's arguments `1` and `0` are set up before the `0x98` store block. Inlines that return one of the arguments, in every argument position, and seven forms of the `+=` expression all give the same hash.
- **New tools (in `$S/ovhimid8/t/`):**
  - `lperm.py base func mod start end` scores every order of the given lines and writes the best one to `base.lp.<ext>`. Use it in place of `hcorder.py` on drafts that use `LFLD(...)`: `hcorder.py` treats `LFLD(` as a call and then finds no legal moves.
  - `boolsw.py` rewrites the flag-style BOOL returns of inlines (`r = TRUE; if (c) r = FALSE; return r;`) as expressions and scores each one. A sweep over 27 near-miss drafts (`bsw/res.txt`) improved nothing beyond `02209948`.
- **Still open** (scored raw / register-masked under dsi/1.3; drafts in `$S/ovhimid8/d/`):
  - `ov009_02206084`: 5 / 5. The target loads `pl->id` before the bitfield `ldrb`. 120 declaration orders and cxxperm did not change it.
  - `021a2670`: 9 / 5. The `b24` word is in `r3`; the target has it in `r2`.
  - `021c8408`: 5 / 2. The target loads the `texBase` stack parameter first.
  - `021b3e90`: 7 / 1 (`a.cpp`, with the compare written as `cam->unk_d6 - 1 != cam->unk_d4`). What remains is our `movne r0, r4` against the target's unconditional `mov`.
  - `ov010_02203f08`: 24 / 11.
  - `ov010_0220c758`: a whole-function register rotation.
  - `ov012_021fab7c`: 6 / 6, scheduling only. The six-byte `rot` copy loads come after `mov r6, r0` in our build and before it in the target. Inlines, a block-local `n` and a field-by-field copy did not move them, and all compilers give 8.
  - `ov010_02200a00`: 2 / 2. Clearing the `0xa4` flags as three bitfield writes does not merge them into one `bic`.
  - `ov013_021fee10`: 0 / 0 by seqdiff but 5 in `match.py` (`ovhimid6/d/fee10/v5.cpp`). The draft reads `anim` through a second declaration of the same symbol (`namespace Kee10 { extern "C" const u8 data_ov013_0220aedc[]; }`). That declaration gets its own literal pool word, and the target has only one word for the symbol. With the second declaration removed (`ovhimid8/d/ee10/a.cpp`), the `anim` load counts as aliasing the stores to `o3` that come before it. The compiler then schedules it after them, writes the `b0`/`b1` bitfield byte out early, and rotates every register (110 / 10). These did not fix that:
    - making the main declaration `const` (18 / 16): the first-block and `x2` loads are hoisted above the `ar`/`o2` stores, which the target does not do;
    - a struct type for the table, or a `const` member;
    - `__restrict` on `o3`;
    - 360 interleavings of `anim`, `b0 = 0` and `b1 = 0` with the other `o3` stores (`d/ee10/g/`, best `match.py` 20).

    The closest single-declaration draft is `d/ee10/q1.cpp` (18 / 13, `match.py` 21), which moves `b0 = 0; b1 = 0;` after the `anim` read.
  - `021bb33c`: 2 / 2. The target's `moveq r0, #0; streq` is not a materialised BOOL: `BOOL k = Keep(self); if (!k) self->fx = (Fx33c *)k;` emits the full `movne #1; moveq #0; cmp`, giving 9 / 9.

## The pooled G3 constants come from an inline asm block (ov000, round strpool2)

Three new matches, each confirmed with `tools/match.py` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, every ROM check OK): `func_ov000_0215b2e4`, `func_ov000_021535a8` and `func_ov000_0215d4b4`, in the new files `src/ov000/G3_0215b2e4.cpp`, `G3_021535a8.cpp` and `G3_0215d4b4.cpp`. Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `strpool2/` (`g3/`, `t/`).

- **The cause.** The four "pooled-G3-constant" functions (`0215b2e4`, `0215b598`, `0215d4b4`, `021535a8`) load small constants such as `1`, `0`, `0x11` and `0x2a` from the literal pool, and they do not CSE them between calls. All four also contain the same 11-instruction sequence: `str x, [m, #0x24]; mov r1, #0x1000; str y, [m, #0x28]; mov r2, #0; str z, [m, #0x2c]; mov r3, #0; stmia m!, {r1, r2}` and so on. This is the asm translation-matrix inline that `TexObj_3.cpp` (`TexObj_SetTransMtx`) and `ObjPool.cpp` (`Box_SetMtx`) already use. Earlier drafts wrote the matrix as a C initialiser. With the asm inline, MWCC loads the constants of the statements around the asm block from the pool, as in the target. `asm {}` and `asm { nop }` did not do this because they never touch a variable. Constants further away from the block are `mov`s again. In `021535a8` the pooled range runs from the function's start to the `MTX_TransApply43` call, and our build gives the same range. The asm block also explains the target's `-0x800` computed as `sub r2, r3, #0x800` from a pooled `0`.
- **Use the matrix inline as it is in `TexObj_3.cpp`.** `021535a8` and `0215d4b4` matched with `G3_SetTransMtx(&t, 0x800, 0x800, 0)` and the parameters in the order `(m, x, y, z)`. Two other changes were needed. In `021535a8`, the alpha field had to be read into a block local before the `func_02043d48` call, because the target loads it before the `bl`. In `0215d4b4`, the draft's argument order had to follow the target: `MTX_Scale43_(&m, sc, sc, 0x1000)`, with both vectors sent before the scale.
- **`0215b2e4` also needs `#pragma optimize_for_size on`.** Without it, our build pools only the constants up to the `0x1c` vector, and it CSEs both `0x1000` arguments of `MTX_Scale43_` into `r4` (120 diffs). With the pragma, every constant up to the last `0x25` vertex is pooled and none is CSE'd, as in the target. But the pragma also moves the matrix pointer into `r4`. Declaring the inline as `(x, m, y, z)` puts `m` back in `r0` and `x` in `r1`. In a small test file under `-Os`, only the orders with `m` second gave `m` in `r0`. The same pragma breaks the other three functions (137 to 182 diffs). So `0215b2e4` probably comes from a different file, or a different inline form gives the same effect.
- **`func_ov000_0215b598` (5 diffs, `strpool2/g3/cv5/120.cpp`, not landed).** It uses the same recipe: the `(m, x, y, z)` inline, no pragma, and the alpha local. These changes also helped:
  - the ternary for `FX_Div` goes in a block local declared before `sx`/`sy` (`s32 k = LFLD(s32, self, 0xd4) ? 0x10000 : 0;`). This also ends the pooled range where the target ends it, before `G3_Cmd1(0x40, 3)`;
  - the scale, the `0x1b` vector and the vertex constants are `0x1000`, not the `0x10000` of the old draft (`match.py` shows this as a pool-word diff);
  - the half-brightness colour is written as `u8 g = (c >> 5) & 0x1f; u8 b = ...; u8 r = ...;`, then `((u8)(g >> 1) << 5) | (u8)(r >> 1) | ((u8)(b >> 1) << 10)`.

  What is left is a swap of the `g` and `c >> 10` temporaries (`r4`/`r2`). About 300 orders, types and forms of the three colour statements and a 7-minute `cxxperm` run did not fix it.
- **`match.py` always reports `bl` against `blx` for calls to Thumb functions such as `MTX_Scale43_`.** Already matched files such as `ov002_021d83f0.cpp` show the same single diff. The linker picks the instruction, and `ninja` passes.
- **String-pool blockers.** `ov003_021e1204` stays at 2 under `opt_propagation off`, `exceptions on`, `optimize_for_size on`, `opt_common_subs off` and `opt_lifetimes off`. Nothing else was changed on the ov003, ov006, TU1 or TU2 pools this round.

## Auditing near-miss drafts for semantic errors (all modules but ov004, round audit1)

One new match, `func_ov000_0219e998` (0x628, a robot message handler; new file `src/ov000/Robo_0219e998.cpp`), confirmed with `tools/match.py` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, every ROM check OK). It came from fixing a semantic error in its best draft. The round checked the best draft of each of 103 unmatched functions within about 40 raw diffs (`splitsweep2/resc.tsv` and `best12.tsv`, plus the 20 newest drafts per function from `ovlo13`, `ovlo14`, `ovhimid5`–`7`, `mainmid8`, `shared3`, `twins1`, `permsweep2/d`, `splitsweep2/sw`/`d`/`ch` and `vol1`, 694 drafts rescored under dsi/1.3). The string-pool blockers (strpool2), the bigfresh9 functions and main TU1/TU2 were skipped. Tables and tools are in the scratchpad under `audit1/` (`best.tsv`, `au2/`, `rc/`, `du/`, `t/`).

- **Three checks, one script each** (`audit1/t/`):
  - `audit.py draft func module` aligns the two listings with registers masked and immediates, offsets and pool words kept. It prints every replaced, missing or extra instruction, and a summary line counting immediate-only, opcode and insert/delete diffs. Pool words are found from the `ldr rX, [pc, #N]` targets and compared unmasked: `match.py`, `sc.py` and `cxxperm` decode pool words as instructions and then mask their "registers", so a wrong pool constant such as `0x19b` against `0x19a` scores 0 masked.
  - `relchk.py draft func module` compares relocations. `bl` targets are compared in order against `relocs.txt`, and the draft's data relocations are looked up in every `symbols.txt` and checked against the raw words of the target function. `relocs.txt` is not complete (`func_ov009_02208b24` loads `FX_SinCosTable_` at `0x02208f30` with no entry), so the ROM words are the reference. `match.py` blanks relocated pool words in both columns, so a wrong global never shows up there.
  - `defuse.py draft func module` traces each source register of an aligned, masked-equal instruction back to the instruction that last wrote it, in straight-line order. A register rotation keeps every def-use link; a wrong variable breaks one. It finds the `0214bcc4` bug from round splitsweep2 (`tst r8, #4` fed by `got` where the target's `r8` is `fl`). Most hits are false positives from scheduling (the same value loaded twice in a different order) or from joins, so read each one.
- **Drafts found wrong.** Do not build on these:
  - `func_ov010_02201bf4`, `ovmix27/1bf4_p10.c` (2 / 0): the third test is `(new_var << 30) >> 31` where `new_var` is already `v << 31`, so it is always 0. The target tests bit 1 of the reloaded byte (`lsl r1, r1, #0x1e` from the `ldrb`). The correct form (`smallsweep1/d/1bf4/a.c`) is 4 / 0. One volatile byte through a `volatile Bits *` gives 9 / 4; non-volatile, struct-copy and `u8` mask forms are worse.
  - `func_ov000_0219e998`, `splitsweep2/sw/func_ov000_0219e998_b1.cpp` (14 / 5): the damage block `if (ok) {...}` sat after the `if (!b0 && p50->a == 0) {...}` block, so it ran when that test failed. In the target (`bne` to the end of the case) it is nested inside it, as round bigsweep1 had noted. Nested, the draft is 31 / 7. Then four steps matched it:
    - `dmg += dmg / 2;` in place of splitmod's `u32` division extraction gives the target's `lsl #15` / `add ..., lsr #16` (26 / 2, `audit1/d/e998/m1.cpp`).
    - `ppa.sh` made the `g` local (the `data_ov000_021ccd54` pointer) a trailing parameter, which fixes the `r0`/`r1` swap of `ok` across the whole case (2 / 2). Without it the best form is 26 / 0.
    - The last two diffs were `moveq #1; movne #0` against the target's `movne #0; moveq #1` for the state test. `if (g->state != 3) ok = FALSE; else ok = TRUE;` gives the target's order; `ok = g->state == 3`, `?:` in either sense, `!(... != 3)` and `if (== 3) ... else ...` all give ours.
    - `ok` stays a scalar (the one-element array `ppa.sh` also added is not needed).
  - `func_ov012_021fab7c`, `splitsweep2/sw/func_ov012_021fab7c_b1.cpp` (6 / 6): the function is one word too long. It reads `data_ov012_0220a3c4` through both the header extern and the `Dup` alias, so the pool holds the word twice; the extra word is a relocation, which `match.py` blanks in both columns, and the target's next instruction is then compared against nothing. The ovhimid1 draft `ovhimid1/d/ab7ch/t_e.cpp` (6 / 6, one pool word) is the right base. Compare the function size with `symbols.txt` when the last lines are pool words.
  - `func_ov010_021fe2a4`, `splitsweep2/sw/func_ov010_021fe2a4_b1.cpp` (12 / 7): the case-`0x14` loop index is `u16`, which adds `lsl/lsr #16` and `blo` where the target has `add; cmp #3; blt` (an `int` index). With `s32 i` it is 13 / 9; what is left is the known second `cmp r2, #0x14` at the switch head.
  - `func_ov009_02208b24`: `< 0x19b` against the target's `<= 0x19a` (already known from round nearfresh1); the score hides it because the two pool words differ only in a "register" of their decoded text.
- **No semantic error found** in the other drafts: their immediates, offsets, pool words, call targets and def-use links agree with the target, and the diffs are register colouring or scheduling. `02006e48` (switch clustering), `ov008_021ce518` (switch root), `ov006_021cf414` (`ldr; cmp; bx lr`), `ov000_021bb33c`, `ov000_021b209c`, `ov010_021fbda0`, `ov000_0218867c`, `0206d43c` and `02068e68` were rechecked by hand and are the blockers described in earlier rounds.
- **`func_ov013_02205c74` (16 / 6):** the two `asr #31` sign words in `Ov013_Dot2` come out late and in the opposite order (the target computes `r1.y`'s first and spills it to `sp+0x10`). All 720 orders of the six `s64` copies in the inline give 16 / 6 or worse (`audit1/d/5c74/grid.py`).
- **Function sizes.** Six best drafts are longer than the target (`02049c80`, `ov000_021aa978` by two words, `ov000_021c4460`, `ov009_021ff908`, `ov010_021fe2a4`, `ov012_021fab7c`) and four are shorter. `sc.py` pads a short draft but not a long one, so for a long draft the target's following function is compared against our tail.

## Loading both subtrahends first, and a menu string pool that spans a whole TU (ov000 below 0x021a0000 and ov001–ov008 giants, round giants2)

One new match, `func_ov000_02189b44` (0x814, a camera path update; new file `src/ov000/Cam_02189b44.cpp`), confirmed with `tools/match.py` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, every ROM check OK). The giants (0x800 bytes and up) left in this range are `02166180`, `02171478`, `021743bc`, `0217558c`, `02176714`, `0217b324`, `02191f18`, `0219d9fc` and `ov003_021cd91c` (bigfresh9). Drafts are in the scratchpad under `giants2/d/<addr>/`.

- **`02189b44`: load the right operands into the difference locals first.** The 5 / 0 draft (`splitsweep2/sw/func_ov000_02189b44_b1.cpp`) had `self->pos.x` and `self->pos.z` in `r3`/`r2` where the target has `r2`/`r3`. Earlier rounds tried 16 spellings, swapped orders and single right-operand splits. What matches is doing the split on both statements and putting both loads before both subtractions:

  ```c
  s32 vx = self->pos.x;
  s32 vz = self->pos.z;
  vx = info.pos.x - vx;
  vz = info.pos.z - vz;
  ```

  Splitting only one of them, or interleaving (`vx = B; vx = A - vx; vz = B; ...`), gives 5 / 0 or worse, so splitmod's singles-then-pairs search never reached it. `giants2/t/hoistsplit.py draft func module outdir` tries this for every run of adjacent `v = A ± B;` statements (each sub-run, right and left hoists). It finds the match from a multi-line version of the old draft. Over the 103 audit1 best drafts it found nothing else, partly because most of them write such pairs inline in an argument or on one line.
- **`0217558c`: a clean draft with real locals is structurally closer (99 / 2, `giants2/d/558c/c1.cpp`).** The old 64 / 5 draft reused the parameter `xi` for `x >> 12` and then `xi += 0x5a`. The target keeps `x` (`r7`), `xi = x >> 12` (`r4`) and `bx = xi + 0x5a` (`r5`, later `bx + 7`) as three values: `add r5, r4, #0x5a` and later `add r1, r5, #7`. With separate `x`, `xi` and `bx` locals the masked diff drops to the scheduled `mov r0, #0x18000` alone, but every callee-saved register is rotated (`self` `r5` against `r6`, `g` and `x` swapped). All 240 declaration orders with `g` before or after `x` give 97–99; `ppa.sh` (`bx` as a parameter plus `x[1]`) and cxxperm reach 50 / 2 and 52 / 2, both with the parameter hacks back.
- **ov000 string pool `0x021cba0c..0x021cbabd` is one object from at least `0x021cba18`.** `"script/"` (`0x021cba18`) is shared by `021763c8`, `021765c8`, `02178fd8` and `021799e4`, `"cem3d1icn00"` by `02176714`, `021782e0` and `0217fd70`, and `"memory.ccmes"` (`021799e4`) runs straight into `0217b324`'s `" "`, `"%d"`, `"DEBUG"`. So `02176714`, `021782e0` and `0217b324` sit in one source file with the matched `021763c8`..`0217fd70` files, and none of them can land with literals until that file is merged (at least `.text 0x021763c8..0x02180350`). `021743bc`'s `"cem3d1sts00"` (`0x021cba0c`) may be its own object.
- **`0217b324` (45 / 14): the target keeps `"%.2d"` in `fp`.** In the hours/minutes/seconds block the target loads the format string once into `fp` and passes `mov r1, fp` to all three `func_0200779c` calls, and reloads the `3600` magic instead. Our build keeps the three division constants and reloads the string from the pool for each call. In a small test file the literal (or an `extern`) is CSE'd into a saved register; inside this function it is not, with literals under `pool_strings`, an `extern`, a block-local `const char *`, an `AppendNum2` inline (string inside or as a parameter), or `opt_propagation`/`opt_common_subs`/`opt_lifetimes`/`opt_dead_assignments` off. One more instruction comes from that, and the rest of the diff is the register shift after it.
- **`0219d9fc` (3 / 2):** the target loads `0x9ae` into `r2` before `0x9ac` into `r1` and compares `cmp r1, r2`. Every form we wrote loads the left operand first, or, with `0x9ae > 0x9ac`, loads in the target's order but compares the other way (`bls`) with `r1`/`r0`. Locals for either side, casts, `volatile`, comma forms, pointer forms, bit-field reads of the two halves, `s16` and a 32-bit read with `>> 16` all give 3 or more (`giants2/d/d9fc/v_a*/`).
- **No change:** `02171478` (11 / 2, the `end`/`index` swap) and `02176714` / `021743bc` (the hoisted-constant rotation of the menu-builder family) were not moved by `hoistsplit.py`; their drafts have almost no split sites.

## Cross-jump direction, a `bool` temporary and the giant leftovers (ov000_021b5578, ov003_021cd91c, ov012_021fd708, round bigfresh9)

Drafts are in `scratchpad/bigfresh9/d/`. Scores are seqdiff raw / register-masked.

- **Cross-jumping keeps the earliest copy of a tail.** In `func_ov000_021b5578` (a 0x848-byte state machine), several cases end in `strb rX, [self, #8]; b end`. MWCC keeps the first copy in the source (or the copy that falls through into `end`), and the other cases branch to it. The target kept the copy in case `0x1e`. The fix is to write the earlier cases as `st = X; goto set;`, with `set: self->state = st; break;` inside case `0x1e`. This fixed the branch layout, from 160 / 28 to about 100 / 2.
- **Smaller fixes in the same function:**
  - The new-node list append wants `Node *tl = self->tail; bool has = tl != NULL; if (has) ...`. A C++ `bool` temporary allocates differently from a plain test.
  - An unsigned `div` for the nibble index comes from a block-local `u8` copy of `e->flag` made before the `SetNibble` call.
  - The target's redundant `beq` is a double null check: `if (r != NULL) { if (r != NULL) ... } }`.
  - A function-scope `BOOL ok = TRUE` coalesces with the hoisted constant `1` (`r8`).
  - Reading `n = self->unk_10->n_ents` before the `func_0206e1bc` call fixes the case-0x10 loop registers. A cxxperm run found this.
- **What is left in `021b5578`: the `data_ov000_021ccd54` load.** The target loads the global once into `r6` before the first call and keeps `t` (the call result) in `sb`. Two drafts are each one step from it:
  - `d/5578_C.cpp` (57 / 0) writes `u8 *g = data_ov000_021ccd54; t = func_0206e7d0(data_ov000_021ccd54[0xca]);`. The code is right, but `g` and `t` swap colours.
  - `d/5578_n.cpp` (5 / 2; the shift inflates `match.py`'s count) defines `g` after the call. The colours are right, but MWCC reloads the global after the call.
  - None of these closed the gap: about 40 single-line forms (inline helpers, a temporary copy, the comma operator, `register`, `const`, `g` or `t` as a parameter or a one-element array), all 720 declaration and placement orders of the head locals, `ppa.sh`, and three cxxperm runs of 15 to 20 minutes. Writing `t = f(g[0xca])` instead of indexing the global changes the whole allocation (140 / 6).
  - Both drafts still use parameter hacks: `u8 f2` (the flag copy) and `Lang *t`.
- **`func_ov012_021fd708`** (`d/d708c.cpp`, 37 / 11; the old `ovhi7` draft was 39 / 13). Writing the case-10 `else` arm first fixed one block. Three blocks are left:
  - The `Ov012_Fx2` inline's `add rX, sp, #0` is scheduled early, which rotates the `r5`, `r6` and `r7` registers. This is the known blocker.
  - The order of the `r14` struct copy.
  - The `lim` load in case 11.
- **`func_ov003_021cd91c`** (`d/cd91c3.cpp`, 86 / 20): the target keeps a non-constant zero in `sb`, copied at the switch head, and also uses `sl`. Every zero local was propagated away, and a two-definition hack made the draft worse.
- `func_ov010_02209948` was drafted here too (`d/9948_landed.cpp`), but ovhimid8 landed it first.
## Struct-field order on a stack argument, a `bool` condition local, and the audited near-misses (round near4)

One new match, `func_ov003_021d36fc` (0x38c; new file `src/ov003/ov003_021d36fc.c`), confirmed with `tools/match.py` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, every ROM check OK). Drafts, permuter output and tools are in the scratchpad under `near4/` (`d/<addr>/`, `perm/<tag>/`, `t/` holds the audit1 scorers retargeted to this clone plus `ablate.py`).

- **`func_ov003_021d36fc`: fill a stack struct in field order.** The ovlo14 draft without the `kq` hack (`ovlo14/d/36fc/nh.c`, 31 / 0) had the hoisted zero in `r4` and `t` in `r5` for the whole function. A 15-minute `cxxperm` run (`--inlines`, 1,103 compiles) reached 0. Ablating its output left one change: the `Ov003DwArg` argument for `func_ov000_0218d398` is filled as `arg.a = 10; arg.b = 0x50; arg.c = 0xffff;` (field order) instead of `b, c, a`. The three stores look the same in both builds; the order only decides which callee-saved register the zero and `t` get. `ppa.sh`, declaration orders and zero locals had not found this. When a register swap covers a whole function, try the order of the stores into a local struct.
- **`ablate.py orig good func mod out`** reverts the line hunks of a permuter result one at a time and keeps each revert that does not raise the score. Hunks that only compile together (a `rename` touches many lines) are kept; finish those by hand with targeted string replacements, as for `36fc` above.
- **`func_ov013_02205c74`: 16 / 6 to 9 / 0** (`near4/d/5c74/h1.cpp`). The first test is written `bool behind = 0 != (Ov013_Dot(&r1, v) < Ov013_Dot(&d, v)); if (behind) {...}`. This puts the two `asr #31` sign words of `Ov013_Dot2` where the target has them. Only this form works. `if ((... < ...) != 0)` and `bool behind = ... < ...;` give 16 / 6. A `BOOL`, `s32` or `int` local is spilled to the stack (258). Do not name the local `near`, which MWCC reads as a keyword. What is left is a register-only swap in `Ov013_Cross` (`r7`/`r8`/`fp` for `d.y`, `d.x` and the first product). Every order of the `a`/`b` component locals in `Ov013_Cross` (222 variants) stays at 9 / 0.
- **`func_ov000_0215b598`: 5 to 2 / 0 by seqdiff, 3 in `match.py`** (`near4/d/b598/min.cpp`). `cxxperm` found that declaring `b` first, as an `s32` (`s32 b = (c >> 10) & 0x1f; u8 g = ...; u8 r = ...;`), puts `g` in `r2` as in the target. What is left is the `c >> 10` temporary: the target puts it in `r4` (`asr r4, r3, #0xa; and r3, r4, #0x1f`) and ours reuses `c`'s `r3`.
- **`func_ov003_021e6274`.** One function-level `u8 n` used for both values in case 2 (`n = self->unk_18; ...; self->unk_93_1 = n; n = n - 1;`) drops the extra `and` before the bit-field store. But `self` and `t` swap (`r7`/`r6`) for the whole function (46 / 6, `near4/d/6274/a.cpp`). In the target `n - 1` reuses `n`'s `r5` and the bit-field byte goes to `ip`. In ours the byte takes `r5` and `n - 1` takes `r6`. `cxxperm` (6,063 compiles) only fixes the swap by moving the store above the `func_ov000_02197dec` call, which changes the order the target has (20). Inlines for the anim tail with `u8`/`u16`/`s32`/`u32` parameters, `n--`, `--n` as the argument and `s32`/`u8` block temporaries give 46 / 6 or the 9 / 5 build.
- **`func_ov009_02208b24`: MWCC derives `0x19a` from `0x66` whenever it can.** In a five-line test file, `VEC_MultAdd(0x66, ...); if (VEC_Distance(...) <= 0x19a)` always gives `mov r4, #0x66` ... `add r1, r4, #0x134`. The target pools `0x19a` and builds `0x66` with a `mov r0` at the call. These all derive it too: literal forms, `long`, `short`, `unsigned` and `long long` types, `FX32_CONST` float expressions, an inline parameter, `const` and `static const` variables, a distance local, every compiler in `tools/mwccarm`, and the `optimize_for_size`, `opt_*`, `peephole` and `optimization_level` pragmas. A two-definition `lim` for the limit gives 29 / 11 in the real function (`near4/d/8b24/k2.cpp`). The same blocker is still open.
- **`func_ov010_02201bf4` (4 / 0).** The matched sibling `02201b48` reads the flags as `v = Ov010TL_V(o); if (!Ov010TL_B0(v) || Ov010TL_B1(v)) return;`, where the macros cast `&v`. In `02201bf4` that form gives the target's registers (`v` in `r1`, the test in `r2`). But it also adds a 4-byte frame (`stmdb sp!, {r3}; sub sp, sp, #4`), which `02201b48` hides in its `push {r3, ...}`. This is the same under every compiler. `register`, struct and union copies, `u8`/`u32`/`s32` reuse of one variable, inline readers and `IsIdle`-style inlines (15 / 12, the result is built as a BOOL) did not help. A 7-minute `cxxperm` run (9,162 compiles) found nothing.
- **Still open, no change:** `ov012_021fab7c` (6 / 6; 15-minute `cxxperm`, 5,657 compiles), `ov003_021d883c` (3 / 2; `VSet`/`VCp` inlines in both parameter orders, and through the non-`const` alias), `ov000_0219d9fc` (3 / 2; the `0x9ac < 0x9ae` compare: written reversed it loads in the target's order but branches `bls`; locals, getters, a `const u16 *` pair and `s32` casts give 3 / 2 or worse).
## Giants in ov000 above 0x021a0000 and ov009–ov015: `h -= K` splits, a signed compare tail and stack-layout scoring (round giants1)

No new matches. All drafts are in the scratchpad under `giants1/d/<addr>/` and were scored raw / register-masked under dsi/1.3.

- **`x -= K` gives `sub; sub` where `x - K` gives a pool load.** For a constant that is not an ARM immediate, such as `0x19a`, any expression form (`a = b - 0x19a`, `f(b - 0x19a)`) loads `K` from the pool and adds it. A separate compound statement `h -= 0x19a;` gives the target's `sub r, r, #0x9a; sub r, r, #0x100`. The matched `ov009_0220189c.cpp` uses the same form. `t/subsub.py` scans built objects for these pairs. This fixed one block in `ov009_02204a10`.
- **A `(s32)` cast on a `u16` compare gives the signed tail.** `if ((s32)self->unk_40->state <= 2 && ...)` gives the target's `cmp; ldrle; ldrshle; cmple`. Without the cast the compare is unsigned (`hi`), and the build has an extra pop path.
- **Score stack layout separately.** `t2/sc.py` takes a new env var, `MASKSP=1`, which also masks `[sp, #N]` offsets. `t/spmap.py file func mod` prints which stack offset of ours lines up with which offset in the target. In `ov009_02204a10` it showed that the target puts the vector locals in exactly the reverse of our declaration order. With the declarations reversed, every object is off by the same 4 bytes. That gap is a spill slot at `[sp, #0x10]`, for the loop-invariant `self + 0x10c` base. Our build keeps that base in `fp` and uses `lr` as a temporary instead, and the target never uses `lr`. Moving the declaration to block, case or function scope, or typing the base as `u8 *` or `Ov009Vec *`, gave the same hash. Without that slot, the best draft is `d/4a10/r.cpp`: 169 / 163 raw and 75 / 40 with `MASKSP=1` (it was 84 / 48). Declaring the base as `Ov009Vec *volatile arr` gives the target's `str r0, [sp, #0x10]` before the loop and `ldr r1, [sp, #0x10]` in it. Every stack offset then lines up, and the score drops to 58 / 23 (`d/4a10/vol.cpp`). It is a lever, not the source: our build then hoists `&v` into `fp`, which the target does not do. Our build uses `lr` as a temporary in the 0x666 FxMul blocks, and the target never uses `lr` in this function, so the original probably has one more live value in that loop. Local type sweeps, `splitmod` and statement orders of the FxMul blocks did not change the rest. Every dsi compiler gives 57 in `match.py`. Reading `d.z` into a temporary before the first in-place FxMul (`lp/pdz.cpp`) gives the target's store-then-load order in that block: 49 raw, but 25 register-masked. The target's second loop reads `self->arr[i].x` as `ldr [self + i*12, #0x10c]` and passes `base + i*12` to `VEC_Distance`. Writing `FLD(s32, (u8 *)self + i * 12, 0x10c)` for the reads and `(Ov009Vec *)((u8 *)arr + i * 12)` for the argument reproduces that.
- **`ov011_021fc0b8` (13 / 3): a constant hoisted at the end of the Find block.** MWCC keeps one constant in a callee-saved register there. With `e->unk_08 = 0` present, the zero takes that register. With that store changed to a nonzero value, or removed, the `1` (`on`) takes it, as in the target. None of these changed it:
  - `opt_propagation off`;
  - member-function inlines and loop forms;
  - zero types and casts, and different `on` forms;
  - inline wrappers;
  - a 1000-second `cxxperm` run.

  `ov011_021fd638` has the same fresh-zero-versus-hoisted-zero blocker.
- **`ov012_02200cd4` (98 / 36).** The target keeps `j = hit - 1` as `sub r7, r5, #1` and sign-extends `j` at the call, so `j` is not constant-propagated. `#pragma opt_propagation off` reproduces this, but makes the rest of the function worse (199 / 37).
- **`021a0d4c` (117 / 13).** The target materialises BOOLs with immediates (`moveq r1, #1`), where our build reuses a register that already holds the constant (`moveq r2, r1`). Types, removing the ternary, and statement orders did not change it.
- **`021b2f08`.** `d/2f08/nk.cpp` (80 / 18) is the bigfresh5 draft with its two-definition hack removed. The `ppa` result `y1.cpp` (75 / 13) declares `y` as `s32 y_1[1]`. `cxxperm`'s best (80 / 10) depends on fake extra parameters. Its clean parts (`unsigned int cnt`, two statement swaps) give the same hash as `nk.cpp`. What remains is an `r8`/`sb` swap across the whole function and the late `movne`/`strne [sp, #4]` of the flag.
- **`021ad8ac` (6 / 6).** All nine placements of the `tgt` assignment were tried, and the base placement is best.

## The menu string-pool TU is blocked by two functions that load no strings (ov000, round menutu1)

No new matches and no merge this round. Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `menutu1/` (`6714/`, `43bc/`, `b324/`, `a1f8/`, `t/`).

- **The TU's extent.** In the pool `0x021cba18..0x021cbabd` every string comes in the address order of its first user, and an unaligned string belongs to the same object as the one before it. `"cem3d1icn00"` (`0x021cba33`, unaligned) is loaded by `02176714`, `021782e0` and `0217fd70`, and `"script/"` (`0x021cba18`) by `021763c8`. So one source file covers at least `.text 0x021763c8..0x02180350` and `.data 0x021cba18..0x021cbac0`. That range holds 105 matched functions in 71 files, plus four unmatched ones: `02176714`, `0217a1f8`, `0217b324` and `0217e560`. `0217a1f8` and `0217e560` load no strings. They only block the merge because they sit inside the range, and a `.text` entry can't skip a function. Until both match, the merge can't land. `021743bc`'s `"cem3d1sts00"` ends exactly on the aligned `0x021cba18`, so the pool doesn't show whether `021743bc` belongs to this TU. If it does, the TU also takes in the unmatched `0217558c`.
- **The TU was not built with `1.2/sp3`/`sp4`.** Those are the only compilers under which `0217a1f8` matches. Under `1.2/sp4`, 81 of the TU's 105 matched functions differ, and `0217e560` has 287 diffs.
- **`0217a1f8` (3): the `-1` derivation is per basic block.** With the two-definition `-1` in its own `if`, the code is `sub r2, r1, #0x79` followed by `mvnne r2, #0`. So only a `0x78` in the same block gets reused. Every one of these still derives it: `optimization_level 1`, every `opt_*`/`scheduling`/`peephole` pragma, the `-ipa file` and `-ipa function` flags with `func_ov000_0217a328` defined in the same file, `s8`/`s16`/pointer/`enum` parameter types, and `~r`, `r - 1` or `r | -1` built from the `vfunc_28` result. This is the same blocker as `ov009_02208b24` (round near4).
- **`02176714` doesn't need the pool merge.** Its one string is loaded at the call, so an `extern` gives the same code as the literal. `t/deadst.py`, which adds a dead store of a constant before each field store, took the splitsweep2 draft from 29 / 2 to 30 / 0. The change is a dead `LFLD(s32, o, 0x84) = 1;` just before `LFLD(s32, o, 0x84) = 4;` on the object stored at `self->0x48` (`6714/ds/l163_1.cpp`). The draft now differs only by a consistent `r4`/`r6` swap: the target has `-0x1ccd` (then `0x80`, then the new objects) in `r4` and the hoisted `1` in `r6`. A second dead-store pass with `-0x1ccd`, `1` and `0`, two-definition blocks for either constant (`t/tdk.py`), dead stores to `self->0x3c` before its first `1` use, and 10 minutes of `cxxperm` from the 30 / 0 draft didn't change it.
- **`021743bc` (114 / 2) can't be matched yet either.** It doesn't need literals: `data_ov000_021cba0c` is loaded once, at its call. The 2 masked diffs are `mov r1, #0x1a` against `mov r0, r8` in the `vfunc_a8` arm (`ternary`, `!=`, a `u16` cast, a value local and a pointer copy changed nothing), and the rest is the known `1`/`0`/`o`/`g` rotation. The dead-store sweep with `1` and `0` gave nothing better.
- **`0217b324` (45 / 14): `"%.2d"` in a two-definition local** (`const char *f = "%.2d"; if ((u32)self & 3) f = "%.2d";`) still reloads the string for each call (44 / 17). A one-element array or a `volatile` local is far worse. This function does need the merge, because only a literal can sit in `fp`.
- **Tools.** `t/deadst.py draft func mod outdir C1,C2` writes and scores the dead-store variants. `t/tdk.py` does the same with a two-definition block around each statement that uses a constant. `t/matchx.py` is `tools/match.py` plus the extra compiler flags in `XFLAGS`.

## A derived pointer local that MWCC forward-substitutes, and the SDK cross-product shape (round near5)

Two new matches, confirmed with `tools/match.py` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, every ROM check OK): `func_ov000_021b5578` (0x848, the bigfresh9 state machine; new file `src/ov000/Lo_021b5578.cpp`) and `func_ov013_02205c74` (0x330; appended to `src/ov013/ov013_02205c64.cpp`, whose delinks range now ends at `0x02205fa4`). Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `near5/` (`d/<addr>/`, `perm/<tag>/`, `t/` holds the near4 scorers retargeted to this clone, plus `var.py`, a parallel string-substitution variant scorer).

- **`021b5578`: hold the derived pointer, not the global.** The bigfresh9 drafts kept `u8 *g = data_ov000_021ccd54` and passed `g + 0xca` to the name builders and `g[0xca]`, `g[0xcb]` to the other calls. Every form of that, with or without the fake `u8 f2` / `Lang *t` parameters, swapped `g` and `t` (57 / 0 or 93 / 0 clean). What matches, clean, is one local for the id array:

  ```c
  u8 *ids = data_ov000_021ccd54 + 0xca;
  Lang021b5578 *t = func_0206e7d0(ids[0]);
  ...
  func_0206dde4(buf, ids);
  ... func_0206e7ec(ids[0], ids[1]) ...
  ```

  MWCC substitutes `ids` back into each use as `add r1, r6, #0xca` and `ldrb [r6, #0xca]`, so the code still looks like a held global base, but the global is now loaded once before the call and coloured as the target has it. The sibling `Lo_021b53f4.cpp` already wrote `u8 *ids = p->unk_ca;`. The comma, one-element-array and brace hacks of the old draft were not needed. When a held base pointer is only used at one fixed offset (plus small neighbours), try a local for `base + K`. A mechanical sweep of this rewrite over the 103 audit1 best drafts (`near5/t/dptr.py`, 9 sites in 6 drafts) found nothing else.
- **`02205c74`: the cross product needs the SDK shape.** The near4 draft (9 / 0) had a register swap in `Ov013_Cross`, which wrote `o->x = ...` directly from `ax`/`ay`/`az` locals. The form used by the matched `ov002_Cross` and `Ef_Cross` (`s32 x = ...; s32 y = ...; s32 z = ...;` straight from `a->`/`b->`, then three stores) matches. Of the variants tried, only this one did: without the `a` locals but with direct stores the result is 20 / 10, and with the `a` locals plus temporaries it is 9 / 0. Before trying statement orders on an inline, copy a sibling inline that already matched.
- **`0217558c` (99 / 2, `giants2/d/558c/c1.cpp`) had a semantic error.** The first `func_02054f10` call takes `self->0x58`, not `self->0x40`: the target reuses `r0` from `ldrne r0, [r6, #0x58]`. The masked score hid it (a register-only difference). Fixed: 98 / 2 (`near5/d/558c/c2.cpp`). Then `g = data_02097d48 + 0x8b0` (the derived-pointer idea) puts `g` in `r4` as in the target (96 / 2, `p1.cpp`), and a one-element `s32 bx[1]` (still kept in a register) puts `self` in `r6` (62 / 2, `arr_bx.cpp`). What is left is `x`/`bx`/the `0x58` model pointer rotating through `r5`/`r7` and the scheduled `mov r0, #0x18000`. No order of the five declarations does better (all 120 tried). `cxxperm` from `p1.cpp` only reached 50 / 2 again, with `bx` as a parameter, and from `arr_bx.cpp` it got no further than 61 / 2.
- **No change:**
  - `ov000_0215b598` (2 / 0): every order of the three colour terms in the `G3_Cmd1(0x20, ...)` argument, both groupings, 125 type combinations and 6 orders of `b`/`g`/`r`, the types of `c`, one-element arrays and a separate `c >> 10` local. All give 2 / 0 or worse.
  - `ov009_02208b24`: ablating `near4/perm/8b24/out-018-0-1240.cpp` (14 / 4) against `near4/d/8b24/arr_v_v3_d.cpp` shows that the four fake parameters (`qx`, `qy`, `off`, `ang`) are what matter, together with the `p_1936` rename that only compiles with them. Without them it is back to 30 / 13 and the `0x66`/`0x19a` derivation. A label or `goto` between the `VEC_MultAdd` call and the compare does not stop the derivation.
  - `ov000_0219d9fc` (3 / 2): `max`/`cur` inline helpers with the arguments in either order (`BOOL` returns materialise the flag, 18 / 16), and `!(a <= b)`, `bool` locals and `== TRUE` forms.
  - `ov003_021d883c` (3 / 2): `x`/`y`/`z` locals in all orders, a struct copy through `*(const Ov003Vec *)&...v`, and `const` pointer forms.
  - `ov010_02201bf4` (4 / 0): the sibling comma form `o->b0 && (v = Ov010TL_V(o), Ov010TL_B0(v)) && !Ov010TL_B1(v)` adds the same 4-byte frame as the macros do (20 / 20). A `volatile Ov010TLView *` read gives 7 / 2, and `u8 v[1]` gives 4 / 0.
  - `ov002_021ec010` (2 / 2): typed parameters, both cast orders, and `d` loaded at each point. A 12-minute `cxxperm --inlines` run (18,834 compiles) found nothing.
  - `ov000_0218a5ec` (2 / 2): the target defines the `0x8000` stack argument in `ip` before the two `ldrh` loads. Every direct-call form (a `u16`/`u32`/`s16` parameter type, inline wrappers) gives 4 / 4. A 12-minute `cxxperm` run only found hacks that score in the wrong place.
  - `ov000_0219badc` (5 / 4): `if (tbl == NULL) return;`, split `if`s, a comma form and if/else for the table pointer. The target hoists `strne r2, [r7, #0xd0]` above `popeq`, and ours does not. A 12-minute `cxxperm --inlines` run (10,978 compiles) found nothing.

## A queued `cxxperm` sweep over the audited near-misses, and a draft with swapped midpoint arguments (all modules but ov004, round perm4)

No new matches. Tables, logs, permuter output and drafts are in the scratchpad under `perm4/` (`cands.tsv`, `resc.tsv`, `best.tsv`, `queue.txt`, `runq.sh`, `perm/<func>/`, `d/96cc/`, `t/`).

- **Candidates.** The drafts in `audit1/best.tsv`, `splitsweep2/best12.tsv` and `resc.tsv`, `giants1/resc.tsv`, plus the newest drafts (up to 10, and 4 permuter outputs) per function under `near4`, `giants1`, `giants2`, `ovhimid8` and `ovlo14` were rescored under dsi/1.3 (854 drafts of 104 functions). 83 functions have a draft at masked ≤ 6 and raw ≤ 30. Left out: the 0 / 0 pool blockers, main TU1/TU2 blockers, the known dead ends from the shared3 and audit1 rounds, functions near5 and menutu1 are working on, and ov004.
- **Sweep.** `runq.sh` runs `cxxperm.py --stop-on-zero` (with `--inlines` when the file has inlines, `-j 1`, 8 minutes) from a shared queue; each worker claims a line with `mkdir claim/<func>`, so the queue can be resumed by removing claims that have no `perm/<func>.done`. Four workers ran 40 functions (2,500 to 8,700 compiles each, on a machine shared with another sweep). Only two improved: `ov006_021e96cc` (13 / 2 to 4 / 2) and `ov012_021fefc8` (23 / 2 to 22 / 2: `v` declared later and `p += side` moved up two lines). The other 38 did not move at all, including every register-only (masked 0) draft in the queue (`0219b808`, `02046070`, `021fb02c`, `0220441c`, `0215e6f0`, `0203a984`, `021512b4`, `021708ec`, `02152f8c`, `021e2490`, `021b628c`, `021de6bc`, `020701d4`, `021d3e14`, `021688c4`, `021ffd34`, `0214dd3c`, `021d1f70`, `021e7ea8`, `021cd744`). Most of these had been through `ppa.sh`, splitmod and an earlier permuter run, and an 8-minute random walk with half a CPU does not get further. The permsweep2 leftovers that had already had a 10-minute run (`021e1204`, `021e51f8`, `02056e74`, `0200d36c`, ...) were queued last and not reached.
- **`func_ov006_021e96cc`: the permuter result was a semantic hint.** Ablating the 4 / 2 result left one change: `u16 ax = a.x;` before the `ov006_MidTo2` call of case 5. The remaining `r1`/`r2` diffs on the two `ldrh` pairs were not allocation. `defuse.py` shows the draft computed `a.y - b.y` and `a.x - b.x` where the target computes `b.y - a.y` and `b.x - a.x` (`b` is at `sp+0`, `a` at `sp+6`). With the arguments the right way round, `ov006_MidTo2(&self->look.y, &a.y, &b.y)` and `ov006_MidR(&self->look.x, bx, a.x)`, plus the `bx = b.x` copy before the first call, it is 2 / 2 (`perm4/d/96cc/s2.cpp`, audit clean apart from the two loads). What is left is the load order of `a.y` and `b.y`: the target loads `b.y` first. Locals for either value in either order, `MidR` by value, `-(*a - *b)`, the `f += 0x2e1` placement and a 10-minute `cxxperm` run (4,438 compiles) leave it at 2 / 2. Without the `bx` copy (or with it after the first call) it is 13 / 4.
- **Use absolute paths with `ablate.py`.** It runs the scorer with the clone as the working directory, so a relative output path scores a file that does not exist and every hunk shows 199998.

## A split subtraction that fixed a load order, and two hidden semantic errors found by a pool-order check (round near6)

One new match, confirmed with `tools/match.py`, the audit (masked 0, no immediate, opcode or insert/delete differences, size 0x590) and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, all 19 ROM checks OK): `func_ov006_021e96cc`, new file `src/ov006/ov006_021e96cc.cpp` with its own `.text` range `0x021e96cc..0x021e9c5c`. Drafts, tools and logs are in the scratchpad under `near6/` (`d/<addr>/`, `perm/`, `t/`, `ss.log`, `ssp.log`, `pool2.log`).

- **`func_ov006_021e96cc` (2 / 2 to 0 / 0): `u16 d = *b; d -= *a;`.** perm4's draft computed the midpoint difference as `u16 d = *b - *a;`, and the target loads `*b` (`b.y`) before `*a`. Writing the subtraction as an assignment and a separate `-=` makes MWCC load the minuend first. One inline `ov006_MidTo(u16 *out, u16 *a, u16 *b)` now serves both calls of case 5: `ov006_MidTo(&look.y, &a.y, &b.y)` and `ov006_MidTo(&look.x, &a.x, &bx)`, with `u16 bx = b.x;` copied before the first call (without that copy it is 14 / 2). Locals, `volatile` and declaration order had not moved the load; the split did at once.
- **A sweep of the split over the other drafts found nothing.** `near6/t/subsplit.py` rewrites each `T v = A - B;` in the perm4 and audit1 best drafts as `v = A; v -= B;`, one site at a time (81 variants); `addsplit.py` does the same for `+` (99 variants). Nothing improved, so the lever is worth trying by hand when two loads feeding a subtraction are swapped, but it is not a common cause.
- **`relchk.py` now checks pool words by address.** The audit1 copy compared reloc targets only as a multiset, and its per-address check was unreachable (an early `return`). `near6/t/relchk.py` runs the per-address check and, where the target has no relocation entry, compares our value with the ROM word, so missing `relocs.txt` entries (most `FX_SinCosTable_` pool words) are no longer false alarms. Run over the 123 best drafts (`near6/t/poolsweep.py`, `pool2.log`), it found three real errors that the masked score hides:
  - `func_ov012_021fefc8` (perm4's 22 / 2): the two table pools are in the wrong order. The target pools `data_ov012_0220a4bc` first, although `data_ov012_0220a4a4` is used first. Taking both pointers with `q` (`0220a4bc`) first, `const Ov012Vec *q = ...; const Ov012Vec *p = ...; q += side; p += side;`, gives the right pool order at 23 / 2 (`near6/d/efc8/c.cpp`). The 22 / 2 permuter result only scored lower because its pool order was wrong. What is left: in the target, `s38` and `side` are in callee-saved `r7`/`sb` and the copy temporaries are in scratch registers; ours does the opposite. Struct assignment, member-wise locals, if/else instead of the switch, `s38` at function scope, and `side` typed as `u32`/`s32`/`int` or as a block local all stay at 23 / 2 or worse. `cxxperm` (8 min, 7,880 compiles) went back to the wrong pool order (24 / 2), because `match.py` masks pool words. Do not trust permuter output on a draft whose pool order matters until `relchk.py` is clean.
  - `func_ov003_021d112c` (15 / 2): `n / d` with a `u32 d` called `_u32_div_f`, but the target calls `_s32_div_f`. With `u16 d` (or `n / (s32)d`) the call is right at the same score. Loading `unk_08` before the `unk_0a` store (`u16 t8 = param->unk_08; o4c->unk_0a = param->unk_0a; o4c->unk_08 = t8;`) gets both loads in the target's order but swaps the two stores (14 / 2, `near6/d/112c/c8.cpp`). The `k2`/`anm` swap (`r8`/`sl`) does not move with the `anm` declaration position or the `k2` type (any type other than `long` gives 114 or worse). An 8-minute `cxxperm` run made no progress.
  - `func_0205e29c` (main TU2 blocker, 9 / 2, not worked on): `data_02149efc` and `data_ov000_021ccd64` are pooled in the wrong order.
- **`func_ov000_0217558c` stays at 62 / 2 (`near5/d/558c/arr_bx.cpp`).** Rewrites tried: `bx = (x >> 12) + 0x5a`; locals for `bx << 12` and `(bx + 7) << 12`; `bx * 0x1000`; `(x >> 12) << 12` for the mask; `x` from an inline or an `FX_Mul`-style inline (61 / 2); a `Model *` local for the `0x58` model; `u32 x`; `g` assigned before `x`. None of them swaps `x` (`r5` in ours, `r7` in the target) with the inline's `model` (`r7` in ours, `r5` in the target).
- **Queued `cxxperm` sweep (perm4's 16 leftovers, 8 min each, `--stop-on-zero`):** none improved, from 3,800 to 9,400 compiles each. These include `021a2670` (16), `021ad130` (28), `021e1204` (2), `021e51f8` (2), `02056e74` (4), `0200d36c` (5), `020459cc` (9), `021c4460` (10), `0206f85c` (14), `02047724` (15), `02153804` (17), `0218e12c` (18) the ov012 trio `021fbec8` (24), `021fc688` and `021fdfa0` (4 each, 1,500 to 2,100 compiles), and `ov006_021d4118` (30 by the permuter's own score; it now parses, and made 3,525 compiles).

## Store order on a copied `u16` pair, the `strne` before `beq` shape, and an audit of 28 near-misses (round near7)

No new matches. Drafts and variant specs are in the scratchpad under `near7/` (`d/<addr>/spec*.py` with outputs in `v*/`, `au/` holds the audited drafts and `sum.log`/`sum2.log`, `t/` holds the near6 tools retargeted to this clone).

- **`func_ov003_021d112c` stays at 14 / 2 (`near6/d/112c/c8.cpp`).** The target copies `param->unk_08`/`unk_0a` as `ldrh r2, [08]; ldrh r1, [0a]; strh r2, [08]; strh r1, [0a]`. With two temporaries MWCC always emits the loads in the reverse of the store order: stores `08, 0a` give loads `0a, 08` (16 / 2), and stores `0a, 08` give loads in the right order but the stores swapped (14 / 2). Plain assignments do not interleave at all (15 / 2). These all stay at 15 or 16 / 2 or worse: every order of three temporaries for `08`/`0a`/`0c` and for `06`/`08`/`0a` (72 variants, `spec9.py`); `u16 *` source and destination pointers, with and without post-increment; a `u16 t[2]`; `u32`/`s32`/`s16` temporaries; a `{u16 a, b}` struct copy, direct or through a local; `volatile`, comma and `const` forms; and the whole copy moved into an inline, with or without the NULL check inside (`spec7.py`, `spec8.py`, `v10/`). The `k2`/`anm` swap (`r8`/`sl`) was not worked on further.
- **`func_ov012_021fefc8` stays at 23 / 2 (`near6/d/efc8/c.cpp`).** All 24 orders of the four function-scope declarations, with `s38` at block or function scope, compile to the same code (48 variants, one hash). In the target `p + side` is computed before `q + side`, but writing `p += side` first gives 23 / 6, and `&p[side]`/`p + side` inside the calls gives a draft four words too long, with the pool words in the wrong order. Moving the `s38->unk_c2_0 = 1` store after the `side` load or the copies makes it worse (32 / 19 or more). In the target `s38`, `side` and the `0xc` stride constant (which `rot = 0xbff4` reuses, as `0xc000 - 0xc`) are in `r7`, `sb` and `r0`; in ours they are in `r2`, `r0` and `r1`. That is still unexplained.
- **Semantic audit (`audit.py` and the per-address `relchk.py`) of 28 drafts at masked ≤ 6 from `perm4/best.tsv`:** no semantic error found. Every immediate, pool word and call target agrees with the target once the insert/delete shift is taken into account (the `BAD ... no target reloc` lines for `ov011_021fc0b8`, `ov006_021cf420`, `ov003_021e6274` and `ov015_0220cbe8` are `bl`s shifted by one slot). What is left is scheduling or colouring:
  - `ov003_021ead50` (2 / 2): the target places `strne r5, [r4, #0x2d8]` before `beq`, where ours has `beq` and then an unconditional `str` (the same shape as the known `0219badc` blocker). Splitting the `if`, `goto`, an empty then-branch and a block-local copy of `tbl` all leave it at 2 / 2.
  - `ov011_021fc0b8` (13 / 3): the target keeps `on = TRUE` in `sl` and materialises the `0` for `e->unk_08 = 0` late in `r6`; ours keeps the `0` in `sl` and the `1` in `r5`. Moving `BOOL on` to the outer block or before the loop gives the same code.
  - `ov010_021ffd0c` (15 / 3): the target interleaves the `Ov010Vec` `ldm`/`stm` between the halfword stores of the `Ov010Rot` copy. With `v` declared first it is 30 / 18. With member-wise copies in three orders it is 34 or worse. Dropping `#pragma opt_unpromotetypes off` changes nothing.
  - `ov000_021b3e90` (7 / 1): the target sets `r0 = ret` without a condition before the `d6 - 1 != d4` test, and ours uses `movne`. `return FALSE`, if/else in both senses, `goto end` and a `BOOL` condition local all give 7 / 1 or worse.
  - `ov015_0220cbe8` (5 / 5): the target holds `p + 0x168` in `r7` from before the first `VEC_MultAdd` call and reads `[r7, #0x418..0x420]`. Every derived-pointer form tried is folded back into `[r4, #0x580]`: a `u8 *` or `Sub168 *` local at block or outer scope, a reference, a `const` pointer, an inline getter, and a C++ member function `get()` (`d/cbe8/spec1.py`, `spec2.py`). This is the one diff that looks like a source-shape difference rather than allocation, and it is still open.
  - `0206e3a0`, `ov010_02200a00`, `ov012_021fdfa0`, `0203d298`, `ov000_021c8408`, `ov009_02206084`, `ov014_022153e0`, `ov000_021ad8ac`, `ov012_021fc688`, `ov010_0220640c`, `ov002_021eca14`, `ov013_021fc7e0` and `ov006_021cf420` differ only by one instruction (or one store pair) scheduled one or two slots away.

## Static initialisers: 17 `__sinit_*` stubs land as their own files (round block1)

The `__sinit_*` stubs had been left out of every sweep on the assumption that dsd could not delink `.init`/`.ctor`. It can: a `delinks.txt` entry may list `.init`, `.ctor` and `.data` ranges and no `.text`, and dsd puts `file.o(.init)`, `file.o(.ctor)` and `file.o(.data)` in the right places of the LCF. The `WRITEW(0)` that ends each module's `.ctor` table lies outside every range. No tooling change was needed for the link. `tools/match.py` now takes the address from a name like `__sinit_ov002_021ef130.cpp`.

- **What MWCC emits.** A dynamic initialiser in `foo.cpp` becomes a local `__sinit_foo.cpp` in `.init` and a local `.p__sinit_foo.cpp` word in `.ctor`. Name the file `ovNNN_<sinit address>.cpp`, then rename both symbols in `symbols.txt` (add `.cpp` and `local`). The renames are in `scratchpad/block1/renames_final.txt`.
- **Why the objects sit in `.data`.** A namespace-scope `const` object with a dynamic initialiser goes to `.data`; without `const` it goes to `.bss`. Every object below is in `.data`, so it was `const` in the original. Use `static const` if nothing else reads it (and mark the symbol `local`), or `extern const T x;` before `const T x = ...;` to keep it global.
- **Whether the stub can be split off.** MWCC addresses a TU's file-local data through the TU's section start plus an offset. If the stub stores at offset 0 of its pool word, the object can be its own file. Give it `kind:data(word)` (or the right size) so it doesn't run into the next symbol. If the stub stores at a nonzero offset, the reloc target is where the TU's `.data` starts, and the object can't be split from what comes before it. A split-off object with other users (`ov006_021ebabc`, whose word `0x021ebbac` is used by `ov006_021d01ac.cpp` through the same symbol; `ov000_021ca80c`, read by `Menu_021c67e4.cpp`) means the original TU also held those functions. When that TU is merged, fold the stub file into it.
- **Landed (17).** Sixteen are `static const fx32 X = FX_Div(256 * FX32_ONE, 15 * FX32_ONE);`, a header constant that each including TU gets its own copy of: `ov000_021ca80c`, `021ca82c`; `ov002_021ef130`, `021ef190`, `021ef1b0`, `021ef1d0`; `ov003_021f76b4`; `ov006_021ebabc`, `021ebadc`; `ov015_0221268c`, `022126ac`, `022126cc`, `022126ec`, `0221270c`, `0221274c`, `0221276c`. The seventeenth, `ov006_021ebafc`, writes the hit radii `{r/4, r}` (`r = 160000 * FX32_ONE`) at `0x021ec484`. It matches only when the inline constructor stores `full = sq; half = sq >> 2;` in that order (`src/ov006/ov006_021ebafc.cpp`). A member-initialiser list, or `/ 4`, makes MWCC load `r/4` and shift it left.
- **What is left, ranked by how much more each needs:**
  1. The three `FX_Div` stubs that store at an offset: `ov002_021ef150` (`+0x10`), `ov002_021ef170` (`+8`) and `ov015_0221272c` (`+4`). Each TU's `.data` starts with string pointers into its own pool, so the stub needs that TU's pool, which is the string-pool merge problem.
  2. Three stubs that copy two words from a `.rodata` table of string pointers into a `.data` object: `ov002_021ef380`, `ov003_021f7820` and `ov011_02207850`. They need the TU's `.rodata`, `.data` and pool.
  3. About 20 `0x48` copy stubs in ov000, ov003 and ov009 to ov014. Each copies a 3-halfword and a 12-byte `const` object from the TU's `.rodata` (base plus offset) into a `.data` object. The `.rodata` bases are also loaded by many `.text` functions (`ov011_022077c0`'s by six functions from `0x021fedc4` to `0x02200aa8`), so each needs a full TU merge.
  4. The large ones: `ov002_021ef1f0` (0x190), `ov010_0220f5dc` (0x24c), `ov013_0220b444` (0x100), `ov012_0220a8d8` (0xc4), and `ov000_021ca6a0`/`021ca70c` (`FX_Div` plus `__ptmf_null`).
  5. Main: `__sinit_0208aa00` sets entry 11's handler of the `Obj3D_2.cpp` animation table `data_0208bc80` to `__ptmf_null`. Each entry starts with a string pointer, so the table has to land with that TU's string pool. `__sinit_0208aa24` builds a global object at `0x0214b0b8` with vtable `data_0208b318` and destructor `func_020396f8` (`ResHandle.cpp`, written C-style), and passes `__register_global_object` the destructor-chain node at `0x0214b0ac`. It needs the vtable's `.data` and the object's `.bss` in `ResHandle.cpp`.

## Static initialisers, round 2: offset stores and copy stubs (round block2)

Eleven more `__sinit_*` stubs match, two of them folded into existing files. None needed a TU merge: the stub file only has to define the `.data`/`.rodata` objects the stub touches, while the strings they point to stay `extern` in the gap.

- **An offset store means one `const` struct with a dynamic member.** `str r0, [r1, #8]` against the TU's `.data` start is not a second object after a table of string pointers. It's one `const` aggregate whose last-but-n field is `FX_Div(...)`: MWCC fills the constant fields statically and the stub stores only the dynamic one (`const Cfg x = { s1, s2, FX_Div(256 * FX32_ONE, 15 * FX32_ONE) };`). Landed this way: `ov002_021ef150` (`data_ov002_021ef5ac`, 6 fields, own file), `ov015_0221272c` (`data_ov015_02212db0`, `{name, scale}`, own file) and the `ov002_021ef170` stub, folded into `ov002_021d637c.cpp` (its only reader) as `__sinit_ov002_021d637c.cpp`. In the folded file, defining the object turns the function's load into a `.data`-relative one; relchk reports it as `<section>`, which is the same address.
- **The `.rodata` pointer-table stubs build a self-referencing struct.** `ov002_021ef380`, `ov003_021f7820` and `ov011_02207850` copy two words from a `.rodata` table into a `.data` struct. The table points back into that struct, at its inline `char[8]` members (`"photo01"`, `"photo00"`). This compiles to the target's stub exactly: `extern Cfg cfg; const char *const tbl[2] = { cfg.photo1, cfg.photo0 }; Cfg cfg = { tbl[0], ..., "photo01", "photo00", tbl[1], tbl[0] };`, with `cfg` non-`const` (it lands in `.data` anyway because it has static parts). MWCC doesn't fold `tbl[i]`. The string symbols inside the struct (`data_ov002_021f0dc4`/`dcc`, `data_ov003_021f8544`/`854a`/`8552`, `data_ov011_02207bec`/`bf4`) were removed, and the relocs to them were rewritten as `to:<struct> add:<offset>`. That includes the gap reader at `0x021f6a14` (`act00`, `+4`). `ov011_02207850` is folded into `ov011_02204b94.cpp`, which owns the table, as `__sinit_ov011_02204b94.cpp`. All five functions there still match, and the table load stays a load.
- **Four `0x48` copy stubs are header constants.** `ov000_021ca77c`, `ov000_021ca7c4`, `ov003_021f76d4` and `ov003_021f771c` copy a zero `VecFx16` and a zero `VecFx32` from `.rodata` into `.data`, and nothing else reads either object. `static const VecFx16 a = {0,0,0}; static const VecFx32 b = {0,0,0}; static const VecFx16 c = a; static const VecFx32 d = b;` gives the stub, both `0x14`-byte sections and the `.rodata+8`/`.data+8` pool words. The second object of each section needs `kind:data(byte[0xc])`, or dsd extends it to the next symbol. Before you take another one, compare its four pool words with the stub's offsets: `ov012_0220aa2c` has the same code, but its vector sits at `.rodata+0x2c` (`0x0220a834`), and the `0x0220a810` in between is read by `func_ov012_02206718`. So it needs the TU's other `.rodata`.
- **Still open:** the other `0x48` stubs, whose objects other functions read through symbols at their `.rodata`/`.data` bases (`ov011_022077c0` and the rest of the list in round block1), plus tiers 4 and 5 from round block1.

## Static initialisers, round 3: copy stubs whose objects other functions read (round block3)

Twelve more `0x48` copy stubs match as their own files: `ov003_021f77d8`, `ov009_02216288`, `ov009_022162d0`, `ov010_0220f828`, `ov011_022077c0`, `ov011_02207808`, `ov012_0220a99c`, `ov012_0220aa2c`, `ov013_0220b544`, `ov014_02216f60`, `ov014_02216fa8`, `ov014_02217050`. None needed a TU merge or a fold.

- **Other readers don't block a split.** The stub file's `.rodata`/`.data` only has to start at the TU's section base, which is the address the stub's base pool word holds, and run to the end of the last object it touches. Define every object in that range. Make the ones other files read global (`extern const T x;` first, since a namespace-scope `const` is internal in C++) under their `data_ovNNN_*` names. The matched readers keep their `extern` declarations, whatever type they use. Objects past the end, like strings the table points at, stay `extern`.
- **MWCC sorts a TU's `.rodata` and `.data` by object size.** It sorts in ascending order, and the sort isn't stable, so the order within one size depends on the whole definition order in a way no simple rule predicts. This sort tells you the original object boundaries. A 16-byte "table" at the section start, ahead of 6-byte vectors, is really four `const char *const` words. `0x0220726e` (ov011) is 8 bytes (`fx16[4]`), not a `VecFx16`, because the 12-byte vector after it starts at `+0x20`. `0x0220b290` (ov013) is a `u16` followed by a 6-byte rotation. `0x02216004` (ov009) is `Ov009Ver[2]`. To get the order right, permute the lines of one size group and compare the section bytes with the ROM, and compare the reloc symbol order too when the objects are pointers. The script is `scratchpad/block3/t/perm2.py` (lines between `//P{` and `//P}`, `rodata=lo:hi data=lo:hi relrodata=sym,...`). Four lines take seconds, seven take too long.
- **Names in `.data` are named `char` arrays, not literals.** MWCC puts every string literal in its own `.data` section, which lands after the TU's objects. A `"nut00"` sitting between two copy objects is a `char x[] = "nut00";` (6 bytes), sorted in with them.
- **Still open:** `ov003_021f7848` (vec32 at `+0x64`), `ov009_022161f8` (`+0x84`), `ov009_02216240` and `ov010_0220f9b4` (seven 12-byte vectors in one group). They're the same kind as the twelve above, but each has a size group too large to permute by brute force. Tiers 4 and 5 from round block1 are untouched.

## Static initialisers, round 4: the size sort is a heapsort (round block4)

Five more stubs match as their own files: the four `0x48` stubs that round block3 left open (`ov003_021f7848`, `ov009_022161f8`, `ov009_02216240`, `ov010_0220f9b4`) and `ov000_021ca678`. None of them needed a brute-force search.

- **How MWCC orders a TU's variables.** It sorts every namespace-scope variable in the TU by size with MSL's `qsort`, which is a heapsort (Numerical Recipes `hpsort`). The input is the variables in reverse definition order. The list mixes `.rodata`, `.data` and `.bss`; each section then takes its own variables in the sorted order. `extern` declarations don't count, and string literals are left out. The model predicted 100 random test TUs exactly. The `.debug_info` order of the `.dwarf.<name>` symbols shows the whole sorted list.
- **Why no search is needed.** The heapsort's permutation depends only on the sequence of sizes, not on which object holds each size. So pick any size sequence, run the heap on the slot indices, and put into each slot the object the ROM wants at that slot's output position (objects of one size can sit in either section in any interleave). Retry random size sequences until the definition order meets its constraints: a copy defined after its source, a `static` defined before its first use, and the `VecFx16` copy before the `VecFx32` copy (otherwise the stub's registers differ). This takes under a second even with groups of 16 pointers and nine 12-byte objects. The script is `scratchpad/block4/t/solve2.py` (definition lines between `//P{` and `//P}`, `before=i:j`). `brute2.py` tries every order and also runs `match.py`; use it for small files, and when string literals or code order are involved.
- **The ROM's size groups show the real objects.** In `ov009_022161f8` the sixteen pointers at `.rodata+0` are sixteen separate `const char *const` objects: one array would sort after the 6-byte vector. `ov003_021f6aa8`/`ab4`/`ac0` are three unnamed 12-byte vectors, `ov003_021f6a96`/`a9e` are `s16[4]`/`s16[5]`, and `ov009_02215ef0` is 6 bytes. Unused objects stay global, so MWCC doesn't drop them.
- **Last objects need a size.** Give the last object of each delinked range `kind:data(byte[0xc])`, or dsd extends it to the next symbol and the delink fails with "not contained within the file's section range".
- **`ov000_021ca678`** is round block2's self-referencing photo block, `{tbl[1], "photo00", "photo01", tbl[2], tbl[1]}`. The table is three separate global pointers plus a `u16[2]`. A string literal in a split-off file is placed *before* the TU's `.data` objects (it gets its own `.data` section, emitted first). So the `"menu/photo"` the first pointer targets (`0x021ca8cc`) stays `extern` in the gap, and the delinked `.data` ends at `0x021ca8cc`. The relocs to the inner names were rewritten as `to:0x021ca8b0 add:0xc`/`add:0x4`, and `data_ov000_021ca8b4`/`8bc` were removed. The stub's load order depended on definition order; all 24 orders were compiled and checked.
- **Still open: `ov000_021ca61c`.** The data layout is solved: one `0x30` struct `{ptr, VecFx16 rot, ptr, ptr, char[8], char[8], VecFx32 pos}` at `0x021ca880`, built from four separate `.rodata` objects. The stub doesn't match: the target stores the three pointers before it copies `rot`, while every variant tried stores the fields in order. The variants were `VecFx16`/`u16`-struct/braced-scalar `rot`, a nested struct, a member with a converting constructor, `const`/`static`/global objects, and every definition order. The best draft is `scratchpad/block4/v61c_final_try.cpp` (10 instructions differ). `ov000_021ca6a0`/`021ca70c` have the same shape, plus an `FX_Div` and a `__ptmf_null` member, so they will probably share the fix. The other large stubs and the two in main are untouched.

## Static initialisers, round 5: the size sort only covers variables defined before the first function (round block5)

No new matches. The experiments are in `scratchpad/block5/`.

- **When MWCC sorts a TU's variables by size.** Round block4's heapsort applies only to the namespace-scope variables defined *before the TU's first function body*. MWCC collects them and sorts them when it compiles that first function, or at the end of the file if the TU has no functions, like every `__sinit` stub file so far. Every variable defined after the first function keeps definition order. Test: four `.data` variables of sizes 80, 4, 12, 2 come out sorted (`2 4 12 80`) when a function follows them. With the function first, they come out in source order. With two of them before the function, those two are sorted and the other two follow in source order. The same holds for `.rodata` (`const`) and `.bss` in C++. In C, uninitialised globals are tentative definitions. Once the TU has a function, they are emitted at the end of the file in reverse definition order, wherever they were written; without a function they are sorted. `@stringBase0` always stays last in `.data`, after the variables defined after the last function. `scratchpad/block5/cc2.sh` plus `dump.sh` compile a test file and print its object order.
- **What this means for the TU merges.** In a TU with functions, the order of `.data`/`.rodata`/`.bss` objects follows the source order of their definitions, apart from a size-sorted run at the start of each section. A section whose sizes rise at the start and then stop rising shows where the first function was defined. For main TU1/TU2 and the ov000 menu TU, the data order is not the problem. Their blockers are register allocation inside six (TU1), five (TU2) and two (menu) functions. `0205e29c`'s "pool order" is the order of its literal-pool words, which follows from the register swap at its top, not from the variable sort. So the model doesn't unblock any of them. It does mean that when those merges land, the `.data` objects can be defined in the ROM's order after the first function, with no solver needed. Objects defined before the first function need `solve2.py`. Round mainpool1's note that "MWCC writes a TU's `.data` as globals in definition order" is right for every object defined after the first function.
- **`ov000_021ca61c` is still 10 diffs.** Round block4 left it there. More things that leave the stub's stores in field order (photo, rot, photoB, photoA, pos), where the target stores the three pointers first: every definition order of the five objects (24 orders, 10 or 11 diffs, so definition order is not the cause); `rot` as flat members, `u16[3]`, `VecFx16`, a struct copy (15), an inline returning a copy (15/25), a functional cast (15), and a member type with a converting constructor from `VecFx16` (10); `rot`/`pos` built by inline constructors or C99 compound literals (the values are folded into static data, so the stub has no copies); five nestings of the struct into sub-aggregates (all 10); `-O4,s`, `-O3`, `-O2` (10); every compiler in `tools/mwccarm` (10 or worse). `ov000_021ca6a0` has the opposite order: the target stores `FX_Div`, `rot`, `pos` and then the three pointers, while a field-order draft (`scratchpad/block5/d6a0.cpp`, 19 diffs) stores the first pointer straight after the `FX_Div` result with an `stm`. In both targets the pointer stores are grouped together and the copies stay in field order. The group comes first when the first dynamic field is a pointer (`61c`), and last when it is the `FX_Div` call (`6a0`). No source form tried gives that grouping. `ov000_021ca70c` has no `rot`/`pos` and stores in field order. It may match once the `ptmf` object at `0x021cc1a0` is understood.
- **The four large stubs are TU-sized.** `ov012_0220a8d8` copies a `rot` and a vector, then copies one stack temporary to seven `0x48`-stride objects (`0x0220ad48`..`0x0220aeb0`). Its `.data` range `0x0220aaa0`..`0x0220aef0` holds about 30 objects (string arrays, `0xac` tables), and its `.rodata` runs from `0x0220a3c4` past `0x0220a474`. `ov013_0220b444` has the same shape, with nine destinations. These need that TU's whole `.rodata`/`.data` defined, which is the same work as a TU merge. `ov002_021ef1f0`, `ov010_0220f5dc` and the two main stubs weren't started.

## Static initialisers, round 6: a pointer-to-member table, and stubs that address each object by its own symbol (round block6)

One more stub matches: main's `__sinit_0208aa00`, folded into `src/Main/Gfx/Obj3D_2.cpp` as `__sinit_Obj3D_2.cpp`. The drafts for the other stubs are in `scratchpad/block6/`.

- **A null pointer-to-member in an aggregate is a dynamic initialiser.** MWCC fills the non-null members of a `const` table statically. A member written as `0` becomes a copy of the two words of `__ptmf_null` in `__sinit`. A pointer-to-member is 8 bytes here: `{function, this adjustment}`. `data_0208bc80` is 18 `Obj3DAnimEnt`s, and entry 11 has no handler. The table's `.data` (`0x0208bc80`..`0x0208bde8`) now belongs to `Obj3D_2.cpp`. The names it points to stay `extern` in TU1's pool.
- **Handlers referenced through a pointer-to-member must be member functions.** The table's relocs need the mangled names, so `func_02052628`, `func_020528ec` and `func_02052bec` are now `Obj3DAnimCls::func_*` members, and `symbols.txt` holds the mangled names (`_ZN12Obj3DAnimCls13func_02052628EiP13Obj3DAnimSlot`, and so on). The handler that reads the owner does `Obj3DAnim *anim = (Obj3DAnim *)this;` first. All three still match, and nothing else calls them.
- **`ov000_021ca70c` is round block4's photo block plus a `{s32, ptmf}` table.** `0x021cc0d8` is `{FX_Div(..), tbl[0], "photo00", "photo01", tbl[1], tbl[0]}`, and `0x021c9d44`/`d48` are two `const char *const` pointers into it (define `d48` before `d44`). `0x021cc1a0` is a table of 42 12-byte `{s32 key; Fn fn;}` entries, and entries 0 and 1 are null. MWCC keeps one `__ptmf_null` copy on the stack and stores it to both, as the target does. The draft, `scratchpad/block6/w1.cpp`, has 13 diffs, all for one reason: our build reaches the table as `.data + 0x24` from the photo block's base register, while the target loads `0x021cc1a0` from its own pool word. Changing the definition order, `const`/`static`, putting a function first, real sizes with padding, or a static member doesn't change that. `ov000_021ca61c` shows the same thing: its `pos` at `0x021ca8a4` (`+0x24` into the "0x30 struct") also has its own pool word, and `021ca6a0` loads `0x021cba00` = `0x021cb9d8 + 0x28` separately. So in all three targets each object the stub writes has its own address constant. In our builds, objects in one section share the section's base. Probably these objects sit in different sections (or TUs) from one another. That is likely the real reason the store-grouping attempts on 61c/6a0 failed.
- **`__sinit_0208aa24` doesn't belong to `ResHandle.cpp`.** The object at `0x0214b0b8` is read by `LangData_3.cpp` (`0x0206e6bc`..), and its neighbour `0x0214b0a8` is read by `LangData.cpp`. So it is the Lang TU's global `ResHandle`, built by an inline `ResHandle() { Clear(); }` (virtual slot `+8`) and registered with its destructor `func_020396f8` (D1; `func_020396a4` is D0). The vtable's address point is `0x0208b318`, after two zero words at `0x0208b310`. A draft (`scratchpad/block6/r1.cpp`, 13 diffs; 8 under 2.0) gets everything but one thing: MWCC stores the vptr to `.bss + 0xc` (the destructor-chain node is at `+0`) instead of through the object's own pool word. This is the same addressing problem as above. Landing it would also need `func_020396f8`/`a4` as a real destructor in `ResHandle.cpp` (`_ZN9ResHandleD1Ev`), plus a `_ZTV9ResHandle` symbol at `0x0208b310`.
- **`ov002_021ef1f0`** copies `.rodata + 0x20` (`0x021f0104` is the base) into word 0 of 93 12-byte entries at `0x021f0878`. It needs that TU's `.rodata` up to `0x021f0128`, and the 93-entry `.data` table with its static fields. Not attempted this round, and neither was `ov010_0220f5dc`.

## Static initialisers, round 7: designated initialisers set the fill order, and a separate trailing vector (round block7)

`ov000_021ca61c` matches as its own file (`src/ov000/ov000_021ca61c.cpp`). The drafts for the others are in `scratchpad/block7/`.

- **MWCC evaluates designated initialisers in the order written.** C99 designated initialisers compile in C++ mode with `-gccext,on`, and MWCC fills the dynamic fields in the order the designators are written, not in member order. That is how the target stores the three name pointers before it copies `rot`: `{.photo = .., .photoB = .., .photoA = .., .rot = data_ov000_021c9824, .photo0 = "photo00", .photo1 = "photo01"}`. Every member-order form tried in rounds block4 and block5 left 10 diffs. With the designators in this order, 5 instructions still differed.
- **The vector after the block is a separate object.** Round block6 was right that `0x021ca8a4` is its own object. It's a `const VecFx32` defined after the block, not a member. As a separate object, its `ldm`/`stm` can be scheduled between the `rot` loads and stores, as in the target. The pool word alone proves nothing, since `ldm`/`stm` always need an exact address. The other 5 diffs went away once it was split off.
- **Definition order without the size sort.** The block (0x24) comes before the 12-byte vector, so the size sort must not run. In the original TU, functions came before these definitions. The stub file uses `static asm void ov000_021ca61c_order() {}` as the first definition. An empty `asm` function emits a zero-size `.text` section and no code, but it still counts as the first function, so the variables after it keep definition order (in `.rodata` too). dsd and the link accept it. The names inside the block lose their own symbols (`data_ov000_021ca894`/`89c` removed). The relocs to them are now `to:0x021ca880 add:0x14`/`add:0x1c`.
- **The size sort and init order are separate.** Dynamic initialisers always run in definition order, including for variables in the sorted group before the first function. Only the layout is sorted. So a group sorted before a function can give an init order that differs from the layout.
- **`ov000_021ca6a0`: 7 diffs, a register swap only** (`scratchpad/block7/best_6a0_7diffs.cpp`). Same recipe: `scale` (`0x021cb9d8`) is its own `const fx32 = FX_Div(..)`, then a struct `{photo, rot, photo1[8], photoB, photoA, photo0[8]}` at `0x021cb9dc` with `.rot` first, then a separate `const VecFx32` at `0x021cba00`.
