When the vector is defined *before* the struct (`m2.cpp`), the code matches exactly apart from offsets, but the vector then lands first. No sorted/unsorted split gives "vector initialised before the struct, laid out after it": the vector is last in the layout, and 12 bytes is less than 0x24. The `r4`/`lr` swap survived every designator order (perm2–perm4 tried 144+120+120 variants), every extern-declaration order (300 tries), and every rodata placement.
- **`ov000_021ca70c` needs its TU's strings.** The photo block at `0x021cc0d8` ends at `0x021cc0f8`. After it come string literals (`0x021cc0f8`..`0x021cc158`), a function-pointer table (`0x021cc158`, probably a vtable), and then the ptmf table at `0x021cc1a0`. The target loads the table through its own pool word because it sits past the string pool, so the stub is not separable without that TU's strings and vtable. `__sinit_0208aa24` is the same case and was not retried.
- **`ov002_021ef1f0` and `ov010_0220f5dc` are TU-sized.** `021ef1f0` copies `.data + 0x20` (`0x021f0124`) into word 0 of 93 `{word, const char *name, s16 -1, ..}` entries at `0x021f0878`. The names are about 90 asset-name strings at `0x021f0128`..`0x021f0878`, so landing it means writing that TU's whole string table into the source. That needs a decision on the string-table rule first. `0220f5dc` (0x24c) copies about 15 vectors and rotations through stack temporaries into more than 10 objects. It is a full TU merge.

## Static initialisers, round 8: an init order that contradicts the layout means one object (round block8)

`ov013_0220b444` matches as its own file (`src/ov013/ov013_0220b444.cpp`). The drafts are in `scratchpad/block8/`.

- **The TU-sized stubs don't need a merge.** `ov013_0220b444` addresses only `.rodata` `0x0220aedc` (the base, `+0x3c`) and `.data` `0x0220b89c` (the base, `+0x30`), and every other object by its own pool word. As in round block3, the file defines both sections from the TU's base up to the last object the stub writes (`.rodata` `0x0220aedc`..`0x0220afd0`, `.data` `0x0220b89c`..`0x0220b998`). The TU's other readers (eight matched files and the unmatched `func_ov013_021fee10`) keep their `extern`s. The `.rodata` is 15 words (12 of them pointers to `char[]` names in the `.data`), a rotation, `s16[4]`, 12-, 24- and 36-byte vector objects, and then seven 12-byte vectors. The size sort stops after the 36-byte one, so those seven come after the `static asm` dummy and everything before it was ordered with `solve2.py` (`before=` constraints keep the init order).
- **The init order showed two arrays are one object.** The stub fills `0x0220b958` (4 spots) before `0x0220b900` (3 spots), but `0x0220b900` comes first in the layout. Defined as two arrays, either the code matches (`b958` first) or the layout does, never both: dynamic inits always run in definition order, and both the sorted group and the unsorted part keep `b900` first. The fix: `0x0220b930`..`0x0220b998` is one `{char name[16]; void (*fn[6])(); Ov013Spot far[4];}` object (`"SHR2"`, a handler table, the four spots), defined before the three-spot array. Both are in the sorted group, and `0x30 < 0x68` puts the array first. Defined as one larger `{near[3], name, fn, far[4]}` object with designators (`.far` first), the stub was 2 instructions off. MWCC scheduled one `stm` differently when both arrays were members of the same object.
- **Symbols.** The stub symbols are now `__sinit_ov013_0220b444.cpp`/`.p__sinit_ov013_0220b444.cpp` (local). The removed inner symbols are `data_ov013_0220b90c`, `b910`, `b920` (in `b900`) and `b940`, `b958`, `b964`, `b968`, `b978`, `b988` (in `b930`). Their relocs are now `to:<object> add:<off>`. `ov013_021ff924.cpp` and `ov013_021fffd0.cpp` read the four spots as `data_ov013_0220b930.far` and still match. Fifteen unnamed `.rodata`/`.data` objects got `data_ov013_*` symbols. `relchk.py` reports every `add:` reloc as BAD because it ignores the addend; the addresses agree.
- **`ov000_021ca6a0` is still 7 diffs (r4/lr).** One more form was tried: `scale` as the first member of the photo struct, with all 120 designator orders (11 or more diffs). The contradiction is the same as in `ov013`: the code wants the vector initialised before the struct, and the layout wants it after. A struct that holds both (the vector as its last member, set by a designator before the others) is the next thing to try. Its address `0x021cba00` has its own pool word, but block7 showed that proves nothing for `ldm`/`stm`.
- **`ov012_0220a8d8` and `ov010_0220f5dc` were not started.** `ov012`'s `.data` range (`0x0220aaa0`..`0x0220aef8`) has three `0xac` handler tables (`0x0220ab44`, `0220abf0`, `0220ac9c`) and seven `0x48` records, about 0x450 bytes. The same recipe applies: write the tables as function-pointer arrays, not class vtables.
## Grid-shape collision tests: both transforms first, then both base adds (ov002, round ovbig1)

One new match, `func_ov002_021e3dac` (0x420, the puzzle-grid test "does this rotated shape leave the map or hit a set cell"; new file `src/ov002/ov002_021e3dac.cpp`), confirmed with `tools/match.py` (0), `audit.py`, `relchk.py`, the size in `symbols.txt` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, 19/19 ROM checks OK). Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `ovbig1/` (`d/3dac/`, `d/41cc/`, `t/climb.py`).

- **Write the two rotated offsets first and add the position afterwards.** The old m2c-style draft (`ovhi16/d2/func_ov002_021e3dac/r1.c`, 150 / 72) wrote `x = (s32)((dj * m[0] + di * m[2]) >> 12) + pos->x;` and the same for `y`. The target spills `pos->y` before the multiplies and reloads `pos` to read `pos->x` only after them. What gives that schedule is four statements, in both loops:

  ```c
  x = (s32)(((s64)dj * m[0] + (s64)di * m[2]) >> 12);
  y = (s32)(((s64)dj * m[1] + (s64)di * m[3]) >> 12);
  x += pos->x;
  y += pos->y;
  ```

  That took it from 122 / 40 to 11 / 11. Splitting only one loop, interleaving (`x = ..; x += ..; y = ..; y += ..`), adding the position first, or computing `y` first are all worse (35–470). This is the reverse of round giants2's hoist (both loads first): here both left operands come first and both `+=` follow. Neither `splitmod.py` (it puts the `+=` right after its own statement) nor `hoistsplit.py` generates it, so try it by hand on drafts with paired `a = expr + base->x; b = expr + base->y;` lines.
- **The last 11 were two spill slots, fixed by declaration order and one `long`.** `cols` and `i` had swapped stack slots (`0x60`/`0x68`). Moving the declarations of `cols` (first) and `i` (after `rows`) matched it. `x` must stay `long`: with `s32 x` the slots reorder again (53 in `match.py`). `long` and `int` are the same size, but MWCC colours them in different groups, so a `long`/`int` swap is a cheap stack-layout lever (it also moved `021e41cc` from 140 to 130 raw).
- **`t/climb.py file func mod a-b [c-d ..]`** hill-climbs single-line moves inside the given line ranges (each round tries every move, keeps the best one). It is a cheaper alternative to `lperm.py` when a declaration block has too many lines for all orders. For example, the 720 orders of the six bitfield reads in `3dac` took 122 / 40 as their best.
- **`func_ov002_021e41cc` (0x3ac, the shape-against-shape version) is not matched.** It has the same structure: two rotation matrices, `i / w` and `i % w` through `func_0200b184`, and an `xa == xb && ya == yb` test. The fresh draft `d/41cc/f.cpp` uses the same split form and has the right instructions apart from stack slots and registers (140 / 132; three climb rounds over the declarations and the head assignments took it to 130 / 101, `d/41cc/f.cpp.cl.cpp`, before the round ran out of time). The target puts `wb` and `wa` in the lowest slots (`sp+8`, `sp+0xc`), with the `s64` matrix copies above them. In the target, `pa` is read with one `ldm` (no split), while `pb->x` and `pb->y` are read apart (split). The best combination of split forms on the old draft was 104 / 66 (`d/41cc/v_sp_sp2.c`).
## Big main leftovers: the printf switch clustering, a main twin list, and fog/const-1 near-misses (main, round mainbig1)

No new matches. Drafts and tools are in `scratchpad/mainbig1/` (`6e48/`, `e3a0/`, `8e68/`, `3100/`, `t/`, `twins_main.tsv`).

- **Scope.** 16 main functions of 0x200 bytes or more are unmatched. Five are TU1/TU2 string-pool blockers (`0204e5d4`, `0204ffdc`, `020508f4`, `0205e29c`, `0205e5c4`). The rest have drafts from earlier rounds; none of them moved.
- **`func_02006e48` (MSL `__pformatter`, 13 / 13) is all switch clustering.** The case set matches the target: every unused slot in the target's `d`..`u` jump table goes to `default`. But the target builds the table over `d`..`u`, with `c` and `x` compared on their own, and ours builds it over `d`..`o`, comparing `s`, `u` and `x`. Things that do not change it:
  - Every compiler under `tools/mwccarm` (`1.2/*` needs `(const char *)` casts on `parse_format`'s returns), `optimization_level`/`optimize_for_size` pragmas, the case-label order within or between arms, and the switch operand type (`int`, `u16`, `s16`, `long`; `s8` and `unsigned` are worse).
  - Extra case labels sent to the `default` arm (semantically a no-op). One extra value anywhere in `h`..`w` makes the table `d`..`x` (7 / 7, so the score drops, but the table is still wrong). Removing a real case moves both tables, so the split depends on the whole case list (a median split), not on local density.
  - `6e48/sw/sw.py` rebuilds the switch alone, with the same arm grouping, and prints the table ranges; it reproduces our `A`..`G` / `d`..`o` split. Single extra values (0x00–0xfe) and pairs of extra values in 0x20–0x7f (`sw2.py`) never give `d`..`u` (about 3,800 builds). The case values or the arm grouping must differ from the MSL source we have in some way not yet found.
- **Main twin list.** `tools/twins.py --modules main,itcm,dtcm` is in `twins_main.tsv`. New pairs worth a look: `0203c2bc` ↔ `0203beac` (cov 0.55; the same GfxSys bring-up, `case 2` of `0203beac`) and `020701d4` ↔ `0207005c` (0.72 / 0.81).
- **`func_0206e3a0` (2 / 2).** The target stores `fogAlpha` before it computes `color | alpha << 16`. These all stay at 2 or get worse: `u32` locals, reading back the stored fields, an `int`-alpha `G3X_SetFogColor` inline, a whole-update inline, and swapping the two loads.
- **`func_02068e68` (26 / 1, `parsweep2/d/m_02068e68/ap/a_114.cpp`).** The masked diff is `rotated = TRUE` in the `rx` branch. The target emits it as `mov r6, r5`, a copy of the hoisted constant-1 register, and ours emits `mov r6, #1`. The `ry` branch is `mov r6, #1` in both. These do not produce the copy: putting a `pop = 1` / `color = 1` definition in the single-predecessor chain above it, dropping the `tbl` two-definition hack, or reading `rx` through `tbl`.
- **`func_02043100` (in-tree draft, 141 / 78).** The target reads the first OAM word three times: `ldm` for the shape and `charNo`, a fresh `ldr` for `x`, and another for `y`. It reuses the `0xc000c000` pool register as `~0xff` (`orrgt fp, fp, lr, asr #22`). The shape-row index is `asr` (signed `int` shape) and the size index is `lsr`. The draft's semantics are right, so what is left is register allocation and scheduling of the prologue block.

## Static initialisers, round 9: record arrays, zero words in `.data`, and folded dynamic members (round block9)

`ov012_0220a8d8` and `ov010_0220f5dc` match as their own files (`src/ov012/ov012_0220a8d8.cpp`, `src/ov010/ov010_0220f5dc.cpp`), using round block8's recipe: each file defines its TU's `.rodata`/`.data` from the base to the last object the stub writes, and the TU's other readers keep their `extern`s. The drafts are in `scratchpad/block9/`.

- **`ov012`: the six destinations are one array.** The stub copies a stack temporary of `0x0220a468` into six vectors `0x48` apart. With six separate records, 9 instructions differ (the scheduler hoists the temporary's load above the previous copy). With `const Ov012Rec data_ov012_0220ad40[6]` (`{effect, id, pos, n, part[2], file, scale}`), the stub matches, and each destination still gets its own pool word. The interior symbols `ad48`, `ad90`, `add0`, `add8`, `ae18`, `ae20`, `ae60`, `ae68`, `aea8` and `aeb0` were removed. Their relocs (the stub's pool words and the four unmatched readers of `add0`/`ae18`/`ae60`/`aea8`) are now `to:0x0220ad40 add:N`. There are six records, not seven: `0x0220aef0` starts the next TU's `.data`.
- **The six rotations at `0x0220a3cc` are separate objects.** As one `Ov012Rot[6]` array, 4 more instructions differ.
- **Zero words in `.data` need `#pragma explicit_zero_data on`.** Each `0xac` handler table is a vtable: two zero words, then 41 handlers. The TU's functions point objects at the handler part, `+8`. The tables are written as `u32 x[2] = {0, 0};` plus `Ov012Fn y[41]`, which keeps the existing `ab44`/`abf0`/`ac9c` symbols. Without the pragma, MWCC puts the all-zero arrays in `.bss`, and the `.data` comes out 24 bytes short.
- **An empty asm function first, then address order.** When the stub's init order matches the layout, as it does in `ov012`, putting `static asm void x() {}` before all the definitions and writing them in address order is all the layout needs. No solver is required.
- **`ov010`: members stored as immediates come from an expression MWCC folds in `__sinit`.** Some vector members are stored as immediates (`0xf619`, `0xffff9856`, `{0x1000, 0, 0}`) while the member next to them stays static (`0x16000`). Several forms fold to static data instead: literals, `static const fx32` scalars, float conversions, `s64` arithmetic, and member reads of `static`/global `const` vectors (MWCC loads those from memory). `static inline fx32 K(fx32 v) { return v; }` around those members gives the target's code, including the `rsb r4, r5, #0x19c00` that builds `0x19c00 - K(0xf619)` from the register that holds `0xf619`.
- **`ov010` needed the heapsort group.** The stub initialises `fa88` before `fa64`/`fa70`/`fa7c`, which come before it in the layout. A 24-way definition-order sweep, with everything after the dummy, found `88, 64, 70, 7c` (match apart from offsets). No order of the 13 objects `fa2c`..`fa88` alone gives that init order and the layout with the rotation at `fa3e` first. With the two `0x1c` segments (`fa94`, `fab0`) added to the sorted group, the order `3e 2c 44 4a 88 5c 56 50 64 70 32 38 7c 94 b0` works. It was found by sampling size sequences with the heapsort model, without compiling (`scratchpad/block9/o10/hs.py`). The `0x34` records (`facc`..`fb9c`, the five that the rodata table at `0x0220ed2c` points to) follow the dummy. The interior symbols `fae8`, `faf4`, `fb1c`, `fb28`, `fb2c`, `fb50`, `fb84`, `fb90`, `fbb8` and `fbc4` were removed. Only the stub's pool words used them, and they are now `add:` relocs.
- **`ov000_021ca6a0`: still 7 diffs.** Round block8's last idea doesn't fix it: one struct with the vector as its last member, with designators, trying all 1440 orders that put `.pos` first or second. The best result was the same 7-instruction `r4`/`lr` swap, and `.pos` first gave 16. `ov000_021ca70c` and main's `__sinit_0208aa24` were not attempted.

## The shape-against-shape test: one pair of offsets per shape, and `u16` widths (ov002, round ovbig2)

One new match, `func_ov002_021e41cc` (0x3ac, the twin of `021e3dac`: "do two rotated puzzle shapes share a set cell"; new file `src/ov002/ov002_021e41cc.cpp`), confirmed with `tools/match.py` (0), `audit.py`, `relchk.py`, the size in `symbols.txt` and a full `ninja` build. Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `ovbig2/` (`d/41cc/`, `t/split2.py`, `t/scan2.py`).

- **The fifth argument is a `Vec2 *` parameter, not a cast copy.** The target reloads the stack argument and stores it back to its own slot (`ldr r2, [sp, #0xc0]; str r2, [sp, #0xc0]`), and the frame is `0x9c`. A local `Vec2 *out = (Vec2 *)e;` adds a slot (`0xa0`). The header prototype now says `Vec2 *out`; the one caller (`021e45c4`) passes `0` and still matches. 130 / 101 to 125 / 103.
- **`u16` widths put `wa`/`wb` in the lowest slots.** With `u16 wa, wb` (the bitfield widths) instead of `int`, the two land at `sp+0xc`/`sp+8` as in the target: 125 / 103 to 67 / 31. `long`/`u32` did nothing. This is the same kind of type lever as `long x` in `3dac`, but here the narrow type is what moves the slots.
- **Head reads in field order** (`ox`, `oy` of `a`, then of `b`), then one `climb.py` pass over declarations and head lines: 40 / 9.
- **The last 9 were one shared `dx`/`dy` pair.** The draft reused one block-local `dx`/`dy` for both shapes. Separate block-local `s32 dxa, dya` (before the `a` transform) and `s32 dxb, dyb` (before the `b` transform) give 0. Declaring the shared `dy` as `s64` also gives 0, but the separate form is the cleaner source.
- **The "both transforms first, then both base adds" sweep found nothing else.** `t/scan2.py` looks through every draft in the scratchpad (39,010 files after a grep prefilter) for 2 or 3 consecutive statements of the form `a = E + p->f;` with the same base `p`, and `t/split2.py` rewrites each run as `a = E; b = E2; a += p->f; b += p->g;`. Only five unmatched overlay functions have such runs (`ov000_02166180`, `0216715c`, `ov006_021e2490`, `021e309c`, `ov012_021fab7c`), and the split made every one of them equal or worse.
- **Leftovers redrafted, not matched:**
  - `func_ov012_021fd708` (`d/d708/v_a1b0.cpp`, 38 / 9; it was 37 / 11). Reading `lim = FLD(u16, tbl, 4)` before `s->timer++` in case 11 gives the target's schedule (the `lim` load between the timer load and its store), but `lim` and the timer then swap `r0`/`r1`. Six types of `lim`, `++`/`+= 1` forms and dropping the local did not fix it. Copying the `r14` rotation field by field, through a pointer, or by assignment did not fix the copy order either. Six forms of the `Ov012_Fx2` inline (`&r` through a pointer, a one-element array, a copy of `v`, the order of the declarations) left the `add r5, sp, #0` blocker as it was.
  - `func_ov003_021cd91c` (86 / 20, the bigfresh9 draft). The target's `sb` zero is a copy of `r5` (the zero used for `pos.z`), made inside the switch dispatch only on the `state <= 0x1f` side. It is stored to `ofs.z` (case 0) and `timer` (case 10). A zero local of any type is constant-propagated (still 86 / 20). `fx32 z = self->pos.z;` at the switch head survives (store forwarding gives a register, not a constant), but it shares `r6` with the original zero instead of getting its own copy (102 / 22).
  - `func_ov010_02209948` is already matched (ovhimid8).
## SDK register inlines, and a dead search loop kept by MWCC (main, round mainbig2)

Two new matches, both from the round mainbig1 twin list. Both were checked with `tools/match.py`, `audit.py`, `relchk.py`, the size in `symbols.txt` and a full `ninja` build (0 FAILED, 19/19 ROM checks OK). Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `mainbig2b/` (`c2bc/`, `d1d4/`, `e3a0/`, `t/pairscan.py`).

- **`func_0203c2bc` (0x37c, the GfxSys 3D/OBJ bring-up; appended to `GfxSys_4.cpp`, which now ends at `0x0203c638`) matched once its register writes went through NitroSDK-style inlines.** The old draft (`mainmid3/d/c2bc/a.cpp`, 116 raw / 4 masked) wrote each register write as a plain `REG32(..) = (REG32(..) & ~m) | v` statement, and `sys` landed in `sb` instead of `r4`. Earlier rounds put this down to register pressure, and parameter, one-element-array, `register`, splitmod and lever sweeps never moved it. Writing the same writes as small `static inline`s that take the value as a parameter took it straight to 0 with no other change. The inlines are `GX_SetVisiblePlane(0x11)`, `GXS_SetVisiblePlane(0x10)`, `G2_SetBG0Priority(3)` and the other `SetBGnPriority` calls, `GX_SetOBJVRamModeChar(0x100010)`, `G2S_SetBG0Control(0, 0, 0xf, 1, 0)` and `G2S_SetBG1Control`, `G2_BlendNone()`, plus `GX_GetOBJVRamModeChar()` for the heap sizes. The body follows the SDK: mask, then `|` the shifted parameter. Lesson: when a function full of GX register writes is register-only wrong, rewrite the writes as the SDK inlines (`include/nitro/gx_*.h` shows their shape) before trying levers. On `func_0206e3a0` (2 / 2), `G3X_SetShading` and an SDK-shaped `G3X_SetFogColor` gave no change, and `G3X_EdgeMarking` with its `if`/`else` made it worse (17).
- **`func_020701d4` (0xf4, `func_02039674` of table entry offset 3; appended to `TblEntry.cpp`, which now ends at `0x020702c8`) has a dead search loop.** The target runs the range search twice. The first result is thrown away, but MWCC does not delete a side-effect-free loop. So the source starts with a bare `Tbl_FindEntry(id);`, and only its loop survives. The second search re-reads the category in its loop condition (`ldr ip, [r2, #4]` inside the loop). `while (Tbl_GetCat(id)->ranges[i].start <= idx)` gives that (18 raw / 0 masked), and the `cat->ranges` spelling hoists the load. The remaining `cat`/`ranges` swap (`r2`/`r3`) did not move for any declaration order or first-call form (about 150 variants). `cxxperm` found a match. Hand ablation reduced it to two things: `cat->ranges` read into a local before the dead call, and an unused copy of `id >> 12` (`s32 unused = type;`). Without the copy it goes back to 18. The copy is not plausible source, but nothing cleaner was found.
- **The ovbig1 lever ("both transforms first, then both base adds") has no main target.** `t/pairscan.py` looked for consecutive `a = expr + B->f; b = expr + B->g;` pairs in every scratchpad draft that defines one of the 45 unmatched main C functions. The only hits are `func_02024730` (`objTrans.x += ... + d.x`, an 85–110 raw draft with bigger structural problems) and a false positive in `getScaleData_`. Neither fits the lever.
- **Other twins with cov ≥ 0.5** are known blockers: `0205c234`, `0204f524`, `0204c158`, `02058f2c` and `02058d10` are string pool blockers, `0206e858` has the shared pool word, `02047724` and `0206f85c` have had every register sweep, `02015510` has the switch tree, `getScaleData_` is at 1, and `__sinit_0208aa24` was left out of scope. None was re-opened.

## Fresh drafts on two old overlay leftovers: a pointer read back from its struct, and the missing frame words (ov000, ov002, round ovbig3)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `ovbig3/d/` (`52e4/`, `0488/`), with `t/md.py` (a register-masked opcode diff that prints target addresses).

- **`func_ov000_021652e4` (0x604, the panel builder: a `w`×`h` tile grid, then parts, then hit boxes) went from 224 / 99 (`bigsweep1/d/ov000_021652e4/base.cpp`) to 185 / 53 (`ovbig3/d/52e4/c3.cpp`).** The hit-box loop already matched; all the gains are in the parts loop.
  - **Read the pointer back from the struct field.** `p->pos = D_POS(D_CNT); pos = p->pos;` (and the same for `oam`) instead of `pos = D_POS(D_CNT); p->pos = pos;`. With the second form, MWCC CSEs the address into base + index (`str rX, [base, idx, lsl #3]`), which ties up a register for the `oam` base and spills `ax` across the second `FX_Div`. Reading the field back gives the target's `str rX, [r8]` / `[r7]` pointer stores and keeps `ax` in `sl`: 255 / 96 to 221 / 73. Writing only one of the two this way is worse (272–283).
  - **Block-local `sx`/`sy` for the two `pos` coordinates** (`s32 sx = acc[c*2] + (ax >> 12); s32 sy = ...; pos->x = sx; pos->y = sy;`): 221 / 73 to 201 / 60. Where `t = data_ov000_021c9a9c[c]` goes in the source makes no difference.
  - One `climb.py` pass over both declaration blocks (`row` after `y0`, `pe` after `p`): 185 / 53.
  - **What is left is mostly the frame.** The target frame is `0x44`, with two words (`sp+0x34`, `sp+0x38`) that nothing reads, between the spills and `tmpl` at `0x3c`. The read-back form makes our frame `0x3c`, so every slot above `0x10` moves. These did not add the two words: a dead `Pos`/`s64` local (address-taken in `if (0)`, or dead-stored), `Pos org` in place of `x0`/`y0`, an 8-byte `Oam` temporary for either template copy, and `long`/`u16` for `row`/`col`/`x0`/`y0`/`j`/`ax`/`i`. The b1 form (pointer first, then the store) has the right `0x44` frame but spills `pos` and `ax` there. So the two unused words may be spill slots left over from an earlier colouring pass, not a source local. The parts loop also still loads `t` and does the `tst #0x40` after the `pos` stores, where the target does them before.
- **`func_ov002_021e0488`: the missing 4-byte slot isn't a pointer copy either.** On the `ovlo12/d/0488/c.cpp` draft (66 / 32), local copies of any parameter (`self`, `list`, `oam`, `count`) behind a `void *` parameter, a `void *mp = m` copy for the call, and a `Vec2 out` for `s32 out[2]` all compile to the same code (same hash). So the "real pointer params" lever from round ovbig2 does not work here in either direction. `u16`/`s16` for `x`, `y`, `val`, `div`, `digits` or `cn` makes it worse (72–86). `s32 out[3]` is still the only change that gives the `0x78` frame (59 / 23).
## Static initialisers, round 10: weak objects get their own pool word (round block10)

`ov000_021ca70c` and main's `__sinit_0208aa24` match as their own files (`src/ov000/ov000_021ca70c.cpp`, `src/Main/Lang/LangHandle.cpp`). The drafts are in `scratchpad/block10/`.

- **Why the target loaded each object through its own pool word.** MWCC reuses one base register for objects in the same section of the TU (`str r1, [r2, #0xcc]`), unless they are more than 4K apart. It addresses a weak object through its own symbol, though. Rounds block6 and block7 blamed the TU's layout for both stubs. The fix is `__declspec(weak)` on the object the stub writes through its own word: the `ptmf` table `0x021cc1a0` and the `ResHandle` at `0x0214b0b8`. Weak objects stay where they are defined in the layout. `symbols.txt` must mark them `weak`, or `dsd check symbols` fails ("expected to be weak but is non-weak"). This doesn't help `ov000_021ca6a0`: there the target reaches the struct from the `scale` base (`ip + 4`), and making any of its three objects weak gives 21 diffs. It is still 7 (`r4`/`lr`).
- **`ov000_021ca70c`.** The file defines `.data` `0x021cc0d8`..`0x021cc398`: the photo block, the TU's eight file-name format strings (`"scpdmy.ccscp"`, `"scp%s.ccscp"`, `"script/"` and so on, written as `char` arrays, which MWCC packs unaligned as in the ROM), a vtable written as `u32[2]` plus 18 function pointers (`0x021cc150`/`158`, needs `#pragma explicit_zero_data on`), and the 42-entry `{key, ptmf}` handler table. The table's 40 non-null handlers are C functions in matched files. So the entry holds a union `{Fn pm; {Fn fn; s32 adj;} raw;}`, the two null entries are `.pm = 0` (a `__ptmf_null` copy) and the rest are `.raw = {func, 0}`, which keeps the handlers C functions. `.rodata` `0x021c9d44`/`d48` come after an empty `asm` function, in address order. Symbols: `data_ov000_021cc0e0`, `0e8` and `1a4` removed, their relocs now `add:`; `data_ov000_021cc150`, `data_ov000_021c9d48` added. `Cmd_02189194.cpp` read entry 0's handler as `data_ov000_021cc1a4`. It now uses `(CmdEntry *)(data_ov000_021cc1a0 + 4)`, which still gives the `+4` pool word and matches.
- **`__sinit_0208aa24`** is `__declspec(weak) ResHandle data_0214b0b8;` with an inline `ResHandle() { Clear(); }`, after `void *data_0214b0a8;` (the TU's `.bss` base). The destructor-chain node between them is the compiler's `@9` (`symbols.txt` names it `@9 ... local`, as dsd checks it by name). The `.bss` range is `0x0214b0a8`..`0x0214b104`, the first main `.bss` delink. `ResHandle.cpp` stays C-style, but its destructors now carry the mangled names that the compiler-generated code refers to: `func_020396f8` is `_ZN9ResHandleD1Ev`, `func_020396a4` is `_ZN9ResHandleD0Ev`. The C functions in `ResHandle.cpp` were renamed to match. `_ZTV9ResHandle` was added at `0x0208b310`, and `data_0208b318` (the address point) is kept for the C readers.
## The SDK register-inline lever has almost no unmatched targets left (round sdkinl1)

No new matches. Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `sdkinl1/` (`t/scan.py`, `t/scan2.py`, `ioscan.txt`, `e3a0/`, `e7ea8/`, `d298/`, `d36c/`).

- **Scope.** `t/scan.py` lists every function in the `_dsd_gap@*.o` objects (197 unmatched functions) whose literal pool holds a word in `0x04000000`–`0x04001fff`. `t/scan2.py` also looks for the address built as an immediate (`mov`/`add rX, #0x4000000`). There are 15 hits. Only two write GX/G2/G3X registers and are not blocked:
  - `func_0206e3a0`: `DISP3DCNT` and `FOG_COLOR`.
  - `func_ov002_021e7ea8`: `MASTER_BRIGHT` and `DISP3DCNT`.
  
  The other hits:
  - `func_02015510`: `VRAMCNT`, the `GX_SetBankForSubBG` switch-pivot blocker.
  - `func_0203d298`: `KEYINPUT`, already in the `PAD_Read` shape.
  - `func_0200d36c`: card `MCCNT1`.
  - The `func_0204e5d4` and `func_0204ffdc` string-pool blockers.
  - ov004.
  - Six DIV/SQRT users (`0x40002b0`) whose diffs are elsewhere in the function.
  
  The `immediate` hits in ov000/ov002 are flag `orr`s, not register addresses.
- **`func_ov002_021e7ea8` (20 raw / 0 masked).** The miss is a whole `r6`/`r8` swap in `case 0`: the brightness value against the hoisted `0x400006c` constant. MWCC CSEs `0x400106c` as `+0x1000` and `DISP3DCNT` as `-0xc` from that constant. Writing the pair as the real SDK inlines did not move it: `GX_SetMasterBrightness(b); GXS_SetMasterBrightness(b);`, with `GXx_SetMasterBrightness_(vu16 *, int)` as in `nitro/gx_disp.h`. Neither did the SDK `G3X_EdgeMarking(FALSE)`. The draft's `kq`/`gx` hacks are needed: a clean `case 0` goes to 90+. Comma, `long` and dummy-local forms also had no effect, and so did 27k `cxxperm` compiles with `--inlines`.
- **`func_0206e3a0` (2 / 2).** The target schedules `strh alpha` before `orr ip, color, alpha, lsl #16`. These all stay at 2:
  - Computing the value into a temporary before, between or after the two stores.
  - Putting the write in its own block.
  - `int` locals and `(u32)alpha << 16`.
  - Taking the register address into a local.
  - A `volatile` alpha store.
  
  Moving the write between the stores gives 7. Storing alpha first or reading through `const u16 *q` gives 5. Neither the earlier `mainbig1`/`mainbig2` variants nor about 15 minutes of `cxxperm` found anything better.
- **Not a register lever:**
  - `func_0203d298` (7) is register allocation in `trig`/`release`. Every operand-order form is 7 or worse.
  - `func_0200d36c` (5 / 0) is the TwlSDK `CARDi_ReadRomWithCPU` loop. The plain SDK `word`/`ctrl` loop gives 8 with the draft's pragmas and 51 without them.
- **Lesson.** The `func_0203c2bc` lever (write each register write as an SDK inline) has been used up on the current unmatched set. Before a new round on it, re-run `t/scan.py` after new functions are delinked.

## Two old overlay near-misses: an operand order that sets the load order, and a constant zero copied into its own register (ov003, ov012, round ovbig4)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `ovbig4/d/` (`a588/`, `0cd4/`). The tools in `ovbig4/t/` are round ovbig3's, pointed at this clone.

- **`func_ov003_021ea588` (0x2d0) is still 17 / 9 (`near6/ssp/func_ov003_021ea588_0.cpp` = `ovbig4/d/a588/a.cpp`).** The only miss is the schedule of the `v` copy from `data_ov003_021f6a80.offset` before the `func_ov000_0219aae8` call. The target loads `offset.y`, then `offset.x`, then `offset.z`. It stores `offset.x` to `v.x` and later overwrites it with `b >> 1`. All of that happens after both `FX_Mul` products, so `a`'s low word lands in `sl` and the push gains `sl` in place of the `r3` pad. Ours loads `offset.x` first. These all stay at 17 / 9 or get worse (50–127):
  - all 720 orders of the six statements (`lperm.py`), and one `climb.py` pass over the declarations and the block;
  - a struct copy, a `VecCopy` inline, a `VecSet(&v, x, y, z)` inline (each of these is 63–127 in every position);
  - a `const Vec *n = &data.offset` pointer, a `self->param` local, recomputing `b` inside `v.x = FX_Mul(..) >> 1` (CSE'd into the call);
  - `long`/`u32` for `a`, `b` and `y`, block declarations moved to the function head, and the `ov012` prototype of `0219aae8` (`u16 mask`, `s16 h`).
  - 12 minutes of `cxxperm --inlines` (2 jobs) found nothing.
- **`func_ov012_02200cd4` (0x8cc) went from 104 / 36 (`ovhi8/t/0cd4.cpp`) to 72 / 27 (`ovbig4/d/0cd4/best_72_27.cpp`).**
  - **Write the source operand first even when it is subtracted.** In the "aim at `0x5c02`" block, the target loads `o->pos.z` before `self->pos.z` but still computes `self - o`. `dz = o->pos.z; dz = self->pos.z - dz;` (and the same for `dx`) gives that schedule: 104 to 98 raw. `-(o->pos.z - self->pos.z)` gets the load order right but adds an `rsb`, and `o - self` (squared, so it is the same value) flips the `sub`.
  - `cxxperm --inlines` (2 jobs, about 15 minutes) then took it to 72 / 25. Its result is full of noise: one-element arrays for `g`, `gm`, `p`, `t`, `hv`, `d`, `kind`, `c` and `dx`; `__typeof__` temporaries; `j = hit - 1` moved below the `!(unk_50 & 0x20000000)` block; seven fake parameters. The fake parameters do nothing: dropping them gives the same hash. Its `unsigned char j` is wrong, because the parameter is `s16` and the value must be -1. With `s32 j` it is 72 / 27, and that is the saved best. No single change was isolated before time ran out. Moving `j = hit - 1` alone does nothing.
  - **The rest is two "constant zero in a register of its own" copies.** In the target, `hit = FALSE` lives in `r5` and `j` is `sub r7, r5, #1`, converted to `s16` at the call (`lsl`/`asr`). So `j` is not a folded constant there. At the tail, `re = FALSE` is `mov r5, r4`, a copy of the `r4` zero that was passed to `0218dbe0`, and `gm` then takes `r4`. Ours folds `j` to -1 and lets `re` share `r4`. These did not reproduce it: `s16`/`u8`/`u16`/`s32` for `hit` or `j`, `(0, hit - 1)`, an identity or `v - 1` inline, a `return FALSE` inline for `hit`, an inline wrapper whose `s16` parameter takes `hit - 1`, and `gm = NULL` before or after `re = FALSE` (106–191). This is the same shape as round ovbig2's `021cd91c` zero copy.
- `ov003_021d8fc0`, `ov015_0220182c`, `ov000_021b9b14` and `ov000_02191f18` were not started. Their best drafts are still the ones in `bigsweep1/bestper.txt` (457, 262, 124 and 341 raw).
## Fresh drafts on mid-size leftovers: a `u16` cast reorders call arguments, and stuck allocations (round midfresh1)

No new matches. Drafts and tools are in the scratchpad under `midfresh1/` (`cands.txt`, `t/var.py`, `b4ac/`, `3f08/`, `9c80/`, `d744/`, `acb8/`).

- **Scope.** 54 unmatched functions of 0x80–0x200 bytes are in the `_dsd_gap@*.o` objects (`cands.txt`). Taking out ov004, the `__sinit_*` stubs, the hand asm (`02075810`, `02076f3c`, `02077118`), the string-pool blockers and the other workers' targets, the worst best drafts were `0203b4ac` (56 / 16), `02049c80` (53 / 22), `0206d99c` (36 / 0), `ov000_0218cfc8` (35 / 2), `ov003_021cd744` (28 / 0), `ov010_02203f08` (24 / 11), `ov010_02204030` (22 / 10), `ov009_0220acb8` (15 / 11) and `ov010_021ff21c` (15 / 12).
- **`t/var.py base func mod START END variants.txt`** replaces the text from `START` to the first `END` after it with each variant (variants separated by `----` lines) and scores each one. It is a quick way to try 5–100 alternative inlines or statements on a draft.
- **`func_ov009_0220acb8`: 15 / 11 to 6 / 6 (`acb8/a2.cpp`).** The target computes the `y + 0x1333` stack argument before it loads `self->f0` for the angle argument. Writing the angle term with a `u16` cast, `func_ov000_02199e94(..) + (u16)((u32)(f0 * 0x4000) >> 16)`, gives that order. The cast does nothing to the value (the term fits in 14 bits), but MWCC still emits an extra `lsr`/`lsl` pair for it, and those are the 6 that are left. `(s32)`, `(int)`, `(u32)` casts, operand order, a `u16` local, and every `int`/`u16`/`s16`/`u32` combination of the inline's return and parameter types and of the prototype's argument types keep the old order (15 / 11). So only a truncating cast moves the argument order, and the cast costs its own instructions.
- **`func_ov003_021cd744` (28 / 0): the allocation is the same whatever the source shape.** The target puts `self` in `r4`. Ours puts the first model in `r4` and `self` in `r6`. These all compile to the same 28: 64 `mask`/`count`/`i` type and order combinations (an `int i` gives 22 raw but 9 masked), an `if` in place of `continue`, block-scoped loop locals, a `self` copy, writing the first `PlayPart` out by hand with its own locals, and `u16`/`u32`/`s32` index parameters or an `if (res != NULL)` form in the inline. Every `dsi/*` compiler gives 28 as well.
- **`func_0203b4ac` (56 / 16): fresh drafts were worse.** A from-scratch draft with block-scoped locals and the clip tests merged with `||` scored 96 / 37. Reading the canvas through `mgr` each time, with no `canvas` local, scored 64 / 28. In both, MWCC still builds the `mgr + 0x284` base. Ours puts the canvas in `sb` and the target puts it in `r6`.
- **`func_ov010_02203f08` (24 / 11).** The target loads `in->z` only after it has stored `x` and `y`. These were all 24–49: a plain `const` inline (no `volatile`), separate `x`/`y` temporaries before the stores, reading `in->x`/`in->y` into locals first, writing `s64` out by hand, `Ov010_VecSet` with the three products as arguments, a braced initialiser, writing through an `s32 *` array, and copying the vector first and then scaling it in place.
- **`func_02049c80` (53 / 22).** The target keeps `-1` in `r4` for the whole function. It returns it (`movlt r0, r4`), compares against it (`cmp r8, r4`) and builds `-0x80` from it (`sub r0, r4, #0x7f`). A `none = -1` local of type `s32`, `u32`, `int` or `long`, used for the first return, the three `!= none` tests and the clamps, is constant-propagated, so it compiles to the same 53.

## Static initialisers, round 11: the weak-object lever on the copy stubs, and what it doesn't fix (round weak1)

Six more `__sinit_*` stubs match as their own files: `src/ov003/ov003_021f7764.cpp`, `src/ov010/ov010_0220f870.cpp`, `src/ov010/ov010_0220f8e4.cpp`, `src/ov012/ov012_0220a9e4.cpp`, `src/ov013/ov013_0220b410.cpp` and `src/ov014/ov014_02216ff0.cpp`. The drafts and helpers are in `scratchpad/weak1/` (`t/rng.py mod start end` lists the symbols in a range with their readers and bytes; `t4/` is round block4's `solve2.py` pointed at this clone).

- **The copy stubs are the weak-object shape.** Each copies a `.rodata` rotation (read from the TU's `.rodata` base plus an offset) and one or two `.rodata` vectors into `.data`. The rotation copy and a `{K(0x1000), K(0), K(0)}` vector are stored through the `.data` base. The copied vectors and their sources are each loaded through their own pool word. That is round block10's rule: make the four vectors `__declspec(weak)` and mark them `weak` in `symbols.txt`. With that, `ov003_021f7764` was 5 diffs on the first draft and 0 after reordering. Each file defines its TU's `.rodata` from the base to the last source vector and its `.data` from the base to the last copy. The strings in between are `char` arrays.
- **Init order against layout.** In `ov003` and `ov010_0220f8e4` the stub copies the weak vector before it stores the `K` vector, but the `K` vector comes first in the layout. Defined in layout order, the constants land in `r4` instead of `lr` (5 diffs). `solve2.py` with `before=` constraints on the init order found a size-sorted definition order that gives both. In `ov012` and `ov014` the ROM's `.rodata` sizes aren't sorted (a 12-byte object before an 8-byte one), so most objects follow an empty `asm` function in address order (`ov012`: only the 3 pointers, the rotation, the first `s16[6]` and the `.data` strings are in the sorted group; `ov014`: everything follows the dummy).
- **Pitfalls hit while landing.** A `const` struct with a pointer member goes to `.rodata`. `ov014`'s `{name, 0x37}` record at `0x022173e0` is in `.data`, so it is non-`const`. A namespace-scope `const` scalar is local in C++. `ov013_0220b410`'s two objects need `extern const` declarations first, or `dsd check symbols` reports "expected to be global but is local" and the readers' relocs come out zero. `ov014`'s ambiguous symbol `data_ov014_02217390` (an `overlays(9,14)` reloc from ov003) lies inside the weak copy at `0x0221738c`. It was removed from ov014's `symbols.txt`; ov009 has its own symbol at that address, and the ROM still matches. The ROTs need a `u16` struct: `s16[3]` gives `ldrsh`.
- **`ov013_0220b410` wasn't a weak case.** It stores six folded constants into the first `.data` words of ov013. `const fx32 a = -K(0x5800); const fx32 b[5] = {...};` (two objects, because a reader loads `0x0220b5a4` through its own word) matches at once. Every `K` form tried gave 0.
- **No help for the long-standing near-misses.** `func_0206e858`: defining `data_0214b104` locally, weak or not, changes nothing. The base draft's address is still CSE'd into `r5` (11), and the two-symbol form is still 1 (an extra pool word). The target reloads one pool word in both calls, and weak objects are CSE'd like any other. `func_ov013_021fee10`: the target loads `0x0220aee0`'s word through `0x0220aedc`'s pool word, while `0x0220af1e` and `0x0220b930 + 0x10` get their own words. So in fee10's TU these are `extern` (one struct-like declaration covering `+0x54`), not local objects. A local definition of the table (weak, non-weak, `static`, `const` or not) in place of the `Kee10` alias gives 92. With the alias it stays 5, whatever the main declaration is.
- **Left: `ov010_0220f958`.** Its `.rodata` base `0x0220f024` is read by about 20 `.text` functions, and it copies a `.rodata` pointer (`[base, #0x34]`), the rotation at `base + 0x38` and the weak-looking vector `0x0220f0a8` into a `.data` TU (`0x0220fefc`..`0x0220ff84`) that holds 13 strings, and it ends with a `0x14` record whose last word is the folded `-0xdb33`. It is the same recipe with more objects. Not attempted.

## Four big overlay functions redrafted from the target: the 64-bit product forms (ov000, ov015, ov003, round ovbig5)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `ovbig5/d/`, permuter output under `ovbig5/perm/`.

- **`func_ov000_021b9b14` (0x824) went from 124 / 40 (`big5/9b14/best120.cpp`) to 94 / 37 (`ovbig5/perm/9b14/out-165-1-1243.cpp`).** The fresh draft (`ovbig5/d/9b14_b.cpp`: real `Obj`/`Model`/`Spr` structs, `Part` padded to 0x28) is 126 / 40. Two small gains: write `q = 0x68` in each branch so it is not folded into `q - 9` (the target keeps it in `r8`), and write block 4's `else` as `if (kind != 0xd) { k = 0x2e000; } else { k = 0x1f000; t = 0x1000 - t; }` with `s64 k` (123 / 39, `d/9b14_x_K1Q1.cpp`). `cxxperm --inlines` took it to 94, but only with fake parameters (`m, w, a, t`) and `u8 *g[1]`. Dropping either one gives 126–140, so neither belongs in a landed source.
  - **The two forms of the 64-bit product are not chosen by the source.** The target uses two forms. The "generic" form has `mla lo(a), hi(b)` with `hi(b)` in the zero register `r4`, then `adc ..., r4`. The "folded" form has no `lo(a)*hi(b)` term and ends in `adc #0`. `(s64)a * (s64)K`, `(s64)a * K`, an inline with an `s32` or `s64` parameter, `K * a`, `s64 k = K` at the top, and a ternary all compile to the same hash. In ours, block 1's `kind == 0xd` path and its `else` merge into one folded product. In the target only the final `>> 12` is shared, and the `else` stays generic. This is the main remaining miss (about 30 of the 94), together with `r5 = 1` being hoisted to the top of the function.
- **`func_ov015_0220182c` (0xa3c) is 262 / 40 on the old draft. 20 minutes of `cxxperm` (1 job) took it to 234 / 44 (`ovbig5/perm/182c/`).** Almost all of the miss is register allocation. In the target, `self` is in `r4` and `0x1000` is hoisted into `fp` (used by `ModelsReset` and the `0x02190cb0` argument). There are only two `1` variables (`sb`/`r7`, `sl` = `ret`; `case 0x50` does `sub r0, sl, #2`). Ours has `self` in `sl`, four `1` copies, and rematerialises `0x1000`. It was not redrafted.
- **`func_ov003_021d8fc0` (457 / 250) and `func_ov000_02191f18` (341 / 184) were not started.** `d8fc0` is a sequence interpreter that copies its 6-byte entry by value at each inline level: the target has six stack copies (`sp+0xc`, `0x54`, `0x4e`, `0x48`, `0x42`, `0x3c`, ...). The old draft's inline nesting does not produce those copies, so a redraft has to begin from the copy count.
## Static initialisers, round 12: the last copy stub, and `ov000_021ca6a0` is five objects, not a struct (round block11)

`ov010_0220f958` and `ov000_021ca6a0` match as their own files (`src/ov010/ov010_0220f958.cpp`, `src/ov000/ov000_021ca6a0.cpp`). The drafts are in `scratchpad/block11/` (`d/` and `a6a0/`).

- **`ov010_0220f958`** is round weak1's recipe with more objects. The `.rodata` `0x0220f024`..`0x0220f0b4` is a 4-byte word, 13 separate `const char *const` name pointers, ten 6-byte rotations, `u8[8]`, `u16[4]` and the weak source vector. The `.data` `0x0220fefc`..`0x0220ff84` is a copied pointer, eleven `char[6]` animation names, the copied rotation (at `0x0220ff3c`, inside the old `ff36` symbol), the weak copied vector, `char[12]`, `char[15]` and a `fx32[5]` whose last entry is `K(-0xdb33)`. The TU's string pool starts at `0x0220ff84`. The sizes rise through both sections, so everything is in the sorted group. With the four dynamic objects in the init order the code needs (rotation, vector, pointer, record), `solve2.py` found a layout-matching definition order in a few thousand tries. The other ~20 readers of `0x0220f024` keep their `extern`s. 24 symbols were added (`f028`..`f058`, `f05c`..`f092`, `ff3c`). `f0a8`/`ff48` are now `weak`, and `ff70` is `byte[0x14]`.
- **`ov000_021ca6a0`: the photo block is five objects.** Rounds block7 to block10 left it at 7 diffs (`r4`/`lr`). The registers come out right only when the vector copy is initialised before the rotation, and the vector sits last in the layout. With the block as one `0x24` struct, that can't work: the sorted group would put the 12-byte vector first, and after the dummy the layout follows definition order. Split into `photo` (4), `rot` (6), `"photo01"` (`char[8]`), the `photoB`/`photoA` pair (`const char *const[2]`, 8 bytes) and `"photo00"` (`char[8]`), the sizes `4 4 6 8 8 8 12` rise all the way to the vector. So the whole TU prefix is one sorted group, and the init order is free. The stub still reaches every piece from the `.data` base, so the code doesn't change. Weak source and copy vectors (`0x021c9c1c`, `0x021cba00`) plus `solve2.py` with `before=` for the init order `scale, vec, rot, photo, pair` gave a match. Symbols `data_ov000_021c9bf8`, `9bfc`, `9c00`, `021cb9dc`, `b9e0` and `b9f0` were added. The `.rodata` pointers now point at the `char` arrays directly.
- **The general lesson.** When an init order contradicts the layout, there are two ways out: merge the objects into one (round block8), or split one object into pieces small enough for the sorted group.
## Mid-size leftovers, round 2: shared function-level locals, and a pooled template that only a named object schedules right (round midfresh2)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `midfresh2/` (`cfc8/`, `f21c/`, `4030/`, `d99c/`, `acb8/`).

- **`func_ov000_0218cfc8`: 35 / 2 to 8 / 2 (`cfc8/n1.cpp`).** The inlined `FindActor` registers line up when the code is written out by hand and reuses the function's own locals. The group iterator is the function-level `group` that the `Group_Remove` part uses later, the actor iterator is the function-level `prev`, and the found group is a separate function-level `g2`. What is left: the target loads the manager pointer before `ret = NULL` (its `mov r1, #0` comes second), and it keeps the found group in `lr` where ours uses `r6`. The found group stays in `r6` whether it is block-local, function-level, declared first or last, or one of 60 random orders of the top-level declarations. Making the actor iterator `next`, or using a block-local one, scores 19–56.
- **`func_ov010_021ff21c` (15 / 12): the rotation is a pooled constant template.** `Ov010Rot r = {0x4000, func_ov003_021d9c38(&d), 0};`, or a copy from the named object `data_ov010_0220ebea` (defined in `ov010_0220f5dc.cpp`) followed by `r.y = ...`, gives the target's schedule exactly (3 / 3). The only difference left is the load offset: the target has `ldrh [r0, #0x2a]` from the blob base `data_ov010_0220ebc0`, and ours has `#0` from its own object. Writing the copy against the blob always schedules it differently: `*(const Ov010Rot *)(blob + 0x2a)`, `&blob[0x2a]`, a `const Ov010Rot[]` view with `[7]` and a `u8 *` local all give 15 / 12, and a struct view with the member at `0x2a` gives 20 / 10 (that holds for six struct and union layouts, `const` or not). Field-by-field copies give 35. So the original was a file-static template in the ov010 unity TU. MWCC addressed it through the pooled section base. A split file can only reach that schedule if this function shares an object with the `.rodata` at `0x0220ebc0`.
- **`func_ov010_02204030` (22 / 10).** Every one of these compiles to the same code: the first block written with the zero stores before or after `z`, `v.x = v.y = 0`, a `z` temporary, `Ov010_VecSet`, the sin/cos values read into locals first, the table index in a local (`k`, `k * 2`, `k << 1`), inline `Sn`/`Cs`/`Rot`/`Pt` helpers, and the SDK `MTX_RotY33` wrapper. The target still moves the two zero stores (from `r4`) after the table loads. Moving `y = c->y` after the call is worse (24 / 12).
- **`func_0206d99c` (36 / 0).** A shared `Save_SetSlots(s, count, size)` inline used for both layouts scores 57 / 8. The register-only diff begins at the first `smulbb`: the target puts the product in `r1` and the device pointer in `r2`.
- **`func_ov009_0220acb8` (6 / 6).** Nothing new was tried here beyond midfresh1's list.

## A sequence interpreter redrafted from the target: comma temporaries and a pointer kept across a switch (ov003, round ovbig6)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `ovbig6/d/`. `ovbig6/t/sc2.py func mod files...` prints raw, register-masked and register-plus-stack-offset-masked scores, and `t/md2.py` is the matching diff. `t/tdis.py ov addr size` disassembles the target with branch targets.

- **`func_ov003_021d8fc0` (0x628) went from 457 / 250 (`ovlo4b/d/d8fc0b.cpp`) to 292 / 154 (`ovbig6/d/best_292_154.cpp`).** With stack offsets masked as well, it is 61 diffs. Of the old draft's remaining diffs, 209 were structural.
  - **The 15 stack copies.** The frame is `0x5c`: 15 six-byte copies of an entry. 12 of them are inline by-value parameters or locals, in source order from `sp+0x54` down: `Wait.e`, `Wait→Goto.e`, `Exec.e`, `Rand.e`, `Rand.t`, `Exec→Goto.e`, `IsCtl.e`, `Run.e`, three `SetPair.e`, `Count.e`. The other three, at `sp+0xc`, `6` and `0`, are compiler temporaries made **after** inlining.
    - A named local in the outer function gets a slot **above** every inline temporary, so it can't be one of those three.
    - Passing `self->entries[self->index]` straight to an inline makes no temporary at all.
    - What does give them is a comma expression: `#define SEQ_CUR(s) ((void)0, (s)->entries[(s)->index])`. That is an assert-style accessor macro, and its rvalue gets a temporary in the late group.
    - The loop `while ((ctl = Seq_IsCtl(SEQ_CUR(self)))) Seq_Exec(self, SEQ_CUR(self));` CSEs both uses into one temporary. That is the target's `sp+6`: the body copies it into `Exec.e`.
  - **The order of the inline slots is still wrong.** The target lays them out depth-first (each nested inline's copy follows its parent's). Ours is breadth-first: all depth-1 parameters come first, then depth 2. None of these changed the order:
    - `#pragma inline_bottom_up on`, `inline_bottom_up_once` or `inline_depth(n)`;
    - C++ member inlines;
    - non-`static` `inline`;
    - a dummy function that uses the inlines first;
    - every compiler version.
    
    A wrapper inline around the whole body is not inlined at all. This accounts for most of the raw-minus-masked gap.
  - **`Run` takes a pointer and copies from it.** `Seq_Run(self, &self->entries[self->index], ctl)` with `SeqEntry e = ((void)0, *p);` inside, and `Seq_SetPair(self, *p)` in cases 3–5. After the flags store, the target reloads the six bytes from the one address computed before the switch (`ldrb rX, [r0, #n]`). A by-value `e`, or re-reading `self->entries[self->index]`, either keeps the bytes in registers (`and #0xff`) or recomputes the address in each case.
  - **`case 0: case 1: case 2: break;` in `Run` gives the 6-entry jump table** (`cmp ip, #5; addls pc`). Without it, MWCC emits a compare chain: 350 to 307 raw.
  - **`Wait` case 3 is `timer = 0; Next(); ClearFlags();`.** That order gives the target's `strh timer` between the index reload and the `cmp`, where `Next(); timer = 0;` does not (297 to 292). The `b4` path keeps `Next(); timer = 0;` (it shares the tail at `0x021d9160`).
  - **The prologue rereads the flags byte for the `ok` test.** Only a `volatile` read gives this, `if (((volatile Seq *)self)->active && self->entries != NULL)`: 321 / 183 to 313 / 171. The best draft has it, but it is probably not the original. A separate `Seq_IsValid` inline, or folding the two tests into one `||`, has no effect.
  - **Left:**
    - the push uses `{r4-r7}` against ours `{r4-r8, sb}`;
    - the final `Seq_CurIsCtl` test computes its address with `mla` from the loop's `entries` register, and the copy that follows computes it again (ours CSEs the two);
    - the `Rand` copy store order;
    - the slot order above.
- `func_ov000_02191f18` was not started (still 341 / 184, `ov000hi9/d/1f18_o3.cpp`).
## Functions that read a static initialiser file's data through its section base (round tufold1)

Five functions match by moving them into the `__sinit` file that defines the data they read: `func_ov010_021ff21c`, `func_ov010_021ffd0c` and `func_ov010_021ffe8c` (`src/ov010/ov010_0220f5dc.cpp`), `func_ov012_021fdfa0` (`src/ov012/ov012_0220a8d8.cpp`) and `func_ov003_021d883c` (`src/ov003/ov003_021f77d8.cpp`). The drafts are in `scratchpad/tufold1/`, and `t/scan.py` lists the candidates.

- **The shape.** The target loads the pool word of a delinked file's `.rodata`/`.data` start and then reads `[rX, #off]` from it. Round midfresh2 showed that a named `const` object gives the right schedule but offset `#0`, and that a struct view of the blob gives the offset but the wrong schedule. Defining the object in the same TU gives both. MWCC addresses the object as the section base plus an offset, and the copy is scheduled like a named object. Objects the target reads through their own pool words, like the `ldm` vector sources and `data_ov010_0220fa88`, also keep their own words when they are TU-local: the word is `section + off`, and `relchk` shows it as `<section>` (or BAD on the addend), but the value is the same.
- **One `.text` range per file.** A delinks entry can hold only one `.text` range, so the functions that move must be contiguous. In ov010, `021ff21c`, `021ffd0c` and `021ffe8c` are separated by five matched files (`ov010_021ff3d0`, `021ff924`, `021ffa6c`, `021ffc98`, `021ffe04`). Those were merged into `ov010_0220f5dc.cpp`, which now holds `.text 0x021ff21c..0x021fff80`, and all 13 functions in it match. The merge needed a few changes: `VecFx32` becomes `Ov010Vec` (because of `ov010.h`), `<nitro/fx.h>` is replaced by `ov010/ov010.h` plus `typedef s32 fx32`, the second `Ov010Shake` struct is renamed `Ov010Wobble`, and `FLD(Ov010Vec, data_ov010_0220ebc0, 0x60)` in `021ff404` becomes `data_ov010_0220ec20`. In ov012 and ov003 the function fits between two existing files, so it is the file's only `.text`. In ov012 the `nitro/fx.h` → `ov012/ov012.h` switch needs casts on 13 handler-table entries whose prototypes `ov012.h` already declares, `data_ov012_0220a3c4` becomes `const u8 *const[2]`, and the interior record names become `&data_ov012_0220ad40[3]`/`[5]`.
- **Order in the file.** Put the functions after every data definition. They then don't change the size-sorted group (round block5), and the stub's code and both sections still match. Each moved entry was placed in `.text` order in `delinks.txt`. dsd's LCF is fine with a sinit file sitting among the `.text` files even though its `.init`, `.rodata` and `.data` come before those of later sinit-only files.
- **What this says about the TU layout.** The ov010 sinit TU's data is read from `0x021fa6a0` (`021fa6a0`/`021fa740` read `ebc0` and `fa88`) up to `021ffe8c`. So its `.text` really spans about `0x021fa6a0..0x021fff80`, the start of the overlay, and every file before `0x021ff21c` is a piece of the same TU. ov012's `0x0220a3c4` TU covers at least `021fab7c..021fe8b4`. `021fab7c` (6/6), `021fc688` (6/2) and `021fd708` read rotations at `+0xe`, `+0x1a` and `+0x14` the same way, but they can't join the file without also taking the unmatched `021fbec8`. In the TU, `021fd708` is 2 diffs: its `x * -0x1000` 64-bit product schedules `adds` before the last `mla`, and four product forms all give 2. Extending the file to `0x021fd708` would work once that is fixed.
- **Scanned, not fixed by this.** `func_ov010_021fe2a4` (12/7) has a real control-flow diff at the top. `func_ov010_0220640c`, `func_ov002_021eca14` and `func_ov009_021ff908` differ in ways that have nothing to do with the data access (register swaps, an extra instruction). `021fbda0`, `02205444`, `02203f08`, the big ov012 functions and main's `func_020459cc`/`func_0204e5d4` were not tried.

## Functions that read a static initialiser file's data through its section base, round 2 (round tufold2)

Five more functions match by moving them into the `__sinit` file that defines their data: `func_ov012_021fd708` and `func_ov012_021fc688` (`src/ov012/ov012_0220a8d8.cpp`), `func_ov003_021e83d0` (`src/ov003/ov003_021f7848.cpp`), `func_ov013_021fee10` (`src/ov013/ov013_0220b444.cpp`) and `func_ov010_02203f08` (`src/ov010/ov010_0220f8e4.cpp`). The drafts are in `scratchpad/tufold2/`.

- **`021fd708`: the two diffs were the branch order, not the product.** Round tufold1 left it at 2, with `adds` scheduled before the last `mla` of `x * -0x1000`. Thirteen product forms (an `s64` operand, `LL` constants, a separate `s64` temporary, swapped operands, a local copy of `x`) all stay at 2. Writing the condition the other way round matches: `if (dd.x <= 0) { flag = 0; x = FxMul(x, -0x1000); } else { flag = 1; }`. The target's `movgt; strbgt; bgt` is the same either way, but the order of the blocks in the source changes how the `else` block is scheduled.
- **`021fc688` matches in the TU as it is.** Its last 2 diffs (`add r6, sp, #0` for `&r` in `Ov012_Fx2`) were the same problem `021fdfa0` had. The best draft (`parsweep3/d/fc688/b.cpp`) matches once it reads the rotation as `data_ov012_0220a3de` and the records as `&data_ov012_0220ad40[2]`/`[4]`. `ov012_021fcf1c.cpp` (`021fcf1c`, `021fcf48`, `021fd148`, `021fd1b0`) sits between them, so it was merged into the sinit file and deleted. The file's `.text` now starts at `0x021fc688`. The merged functions are in the handler tables, so their prototypes become `(Ov012Actor *)` and the table entries are cast to `Ov012Fn`.
- **`021fbec8` does not match in the TU.** It stays at 24 / 0, the same `px`/`lim` register swap described in earlier rounds. It is the only thing between `ov012_021fb818.cpp` and the sinit file, so `021fab7c` cannot join yet.
- **`ov003_021e83d0`, `ov010_02203f08` and `ov013_021fee10` were new scanner hits.** Each one matched the first time it was compiled in its TU. In `021e83d0` the vector becomes `v = data_ov003_021f6ae4;`, a plain copy of the TU's static zero vector, so the `volatile` trick is no longer needed. In `02203f08` the scaled vector is `data_ov010_0220fe38`, read by name. In `fee10` the animation name is `data_ov013_0220aee0` and the position is `data_ov013_0220af28`. Inside the TU the compiler merges the second read of `af28.x` with the first, but the target reads it again, so that read goes through `(volatile VecFx32 *)`. To switch includes in ov013 and ov010, replace `<nitro/fx.h>` with the module header and add a typedef: `typedef Ov013Vec VecFx32` in ov013, `typedef s32 fx32` in ov010 (where `VecFx32` also becomes `Ov010Vec`).
- **Where the entries go.** Each moved sinit entry sits directly after the file whose `.text` ends where the new range starts. The `.init`/`.rodata`/`.data` of those entries now come before other sinit files' sections in `delinks.txt`, and the ROM still matches.
- **The scanner, round 2.** `t/scan2.py` also tracks pool words that point inside a sinit file's range, and counts only `[r, #off]` accesses that land on a different symbol. It adds `ov010_02201064` (176 / 6), `ov010_0220c758` (a whole-function register rotation; it reads `0220f54c` through that object's own word, so this was a false hit) and `ov003_021ea588` (perm5's target). In `func_020459cc` the `.bss` hit is a false one: the scanner does not clear the register on `ldr r0, [r0]`, and the draft was already compiled inside `Wh.cpp` (9).
- **Tried, no change:** `ov010_02205444` in `ov010_0220f958.cpp` (26, the same `strb`/`ldrh` order and case-10 diffs). Not tried: `ov010_021fbda0`, which would need `ov010_0220f5dc.cpp` extended down past `021fe2a4` (12 / 7, a real control-flow diff); `ov011_021ff24c` (96 / 10); `ov009_02204a10`; and `0204e5d4` (string pool).

## Permuter pass on the drafts that recently got closer: only one-element arrays and fake parameters help (round perm5)

No new matches. Drafts, outputs and tools are in the scratchpad under `perm5/` (`d/`, `out/`, `t/`). `t/vv.py base func mod outdir name:old=>new|||old=>new ...` scores text-replacement variants. `t/dearr.py in out var...` turns `T v[1]` back into a scalar (and `nop` drops the permuter's `_pNNNN` dummies). Use these to ablate a permuter result by concept when line-hunk ablation (`t/ablate.py`) can't, because the array edits are spread over many lines.

- **About 8 minutes of `cxxperm` each with 1 job (`--inlines` where the file has inlines). These found nothing:**
  - `ov009_0220acb8` (6 / 6), twice;
  - `ov003_021d112c` (14 / 2);
  - `ov003_021ea588` (17 / 9);
  - `ov003_021ead50` (2 / 2);
  - `ov012_02200cd4` (72 / 27);
  - `0203d298` (7 / 2);
  - `0200d36c` (5 / 0);
  - `0206e3a0` (2 / 2);
  - `ov002_021e7ea8`. `sdkinl1/e7ea8/b.cpp` scores 28 / 0 with `sc.py`, not 20; 20 is the positional count.
- **`func_ov000_0218cfc8` (8 / 2, `midfresh2/cfc8/n1.cpp`) can reach 3 / 2 (`perm5/d/cv/m2.cpp`), but not in a form that can land.** The gain needs `Actor *ret[1]` and `Actor *next[1]`. With `ret` as a scalar it is 33, and with `next` as a scalar it is 11. The permuter's other edits do nothing: `0xf000 == key`, `(0, ...)`, `g2` moved into the block, the declaration order, and the dummies. The last miss is one scratch register: the target loads `data_ov000_021ccd68` through `r1` before `ret = NULL` (`mov r1, #0`), while ours uses `r3`. A `mgr` local loaded before `ret = NULL`, either order of the `id`/`ret` declarations, and calling the header's `ActorMgr_FindActor` (46 / 5) did not fix it. A second 7-minute run on `m2.cpp` found nothing either.
- **`func_ov000_021652e4` (185 / 53).** `cxxperm` reached 91 / 46 (`out/52e4/out-139-0-1253.cpp`). That needs seven fake parameters (`col`, `j` and `h` matter) and `row[1]`, `i[1]` and `pe[1]` arrays. With those taken out it is 159 / 56. Line-ablated against the old draft, the real edits give 156 / 56 (`d/c52/clean_ab.cpp`):
  - `k` and `rx` declared at function scope;
  - `t = tbl[...]` moved after the two `pos[k]` stores;
  - the part loop's `t` load after `pos->y = sy`;
  - `pe` advanced after `D_CNT++`;
  - `hy` assigned separately.
  
  That is fewer raw diffs but more masked ones. A second run from that draft reached 126 / 49, again only with fake parameters and arrays.
- **Lesson.** On these drafts the permuter's gains come only from one-element arrays and fake parameters. Both change which virtual register MWCC numbers first, not the code. A draft that only improves through them is stuck on register allocation that the source shape doesn't control, so don't permute it again without a structural redraft.
## Functions that read a static initialiser file's data through its section base, round 3 (round tufold3)

`func_ov003_021ea588` matches in `src/ov003/ov003_021f7848.cpp`. The drafts are in `scratchpad/tufold3/` (`a588/`, `bec8/`, `1064/`, `f24c/`, `e2a4/`), and `t/v.py base func mod variants.txt` scores literal-text variants (`OLD\n----\nNEW` pairs joined by `%%%%`, variants separated by `====`).

- **`021ea588`: in the TU the copy order is free.** Rounds ovlo12 to ovbig4 left it at 17 / 9, with the `offset.x` load scheduled too early. The offset is `data_ov003_021f6ae4`, the TU's static zero vector, which round tufold2 found in `021e83d0`. Compiled in the TU, both `FxMul` products written first and then `v = data_ov003_021f6ae4` (as a struct copy or as three field copies), followed by `v.x = b >> 1`, match. The original statement order, with `v.x` between the two products, is 23. A copy written before either product, or through the `VecCopy` inline, gives 140 or more.
- **A seven-file merge, in namespaces.** Between the TU's `.text` (`021e83d0..021e8544`) and `021ea588` there were seven matched files: `ov003_021e8544.cpp`, `021e8a70.cpp`, `021e8eb0.cpp`, `021e93d8.cpp`, `021e9af4.c`, `021e9b74.cpp` and `021ea3d0.c`. Each one went into `ov003_021f7848.cpp` unchanged, wrapped in its own `namespace m_<addr> { ... }`, and the two `.c` files were also wrapped in `extern "C" {}`. The namespaces keep the files' clashing local typedefs, inlines and differently typed `extern` declarations apart. MWCC accepts the same `extern "C"` name declared with different types in different namespaces. The functions keep their C names, and all 21 functions in `0x021e83d0..0x021ea858` match. `021ea234` shows 7 `bl`/`blx` differences in `match.py` as a `.cpp`, just as it does as a `.c` (they are Thumb calls resolved at link time). This is much quicker than reconciling the types by hand.
- **`ov012_021fbec8` is still 24 / 0 in the TU.** Both swaps stay: `px`/`lim` in case 1 (`r4`/`r6`) and the timer/`unk_04` pair in `Bec8_S11` (`r1`/`r2`). These all stay at 24 (or get worse): the branch inversion that fixed `021fd708` (`if (t > lim)` first, 56), the `lim` switch rewritten as `if`/`else if` or with the cases swapped, reordered `unk_b8` stores, `FALSE` for the zero arguments, an `Ov012_Fx1` spawn inline (like the TU's `Ov012_Fx2`) in `S1` alone or in `S1` and `S14`, a zero parameter for the four zero arguments (all 15 subsets), extra `lim`/zero parameters, writing `S1` out inside the `case` (69), and seven forms of the `S11` test (`s32 w`, `w + 0x28 >= timer`, `++s->timer` into a local, `w` read first, `w + 0x28` precomputed). So `021fab7c` still cannot join the file.
- **No TU effect on the other targets.** `ov010_02201064` compiled after the data of `ov010_0220f828.cpp` is still 108 raw / 6 masked: scan2's `ldrb [r, #0xc]` hit was a register-reuse false positive, and the last diff is the fresh `mov r1, #0` for the `last` stores. A store-and-test inline taking `last`, chained stores and stores copied from the last field did not change it. `ov011_021ff24c` in `ov011_022077c0.cpp` with `data_ov011_02207278.x/y/z` (the blob's `+0x20`) is 114 / 6, the same as outside. `ov010_021fe2a4` inside `ov010_0220f5dc.cpp` is worse (401 raw): the TU-local `data_ov010_0220ecb0` is CSE'd into `r5`, while the target loads its one pool word twice. The draft's `namespace Dup` extern trick reproduces the reload outside the TU but would give a second pool word inside it. `ov009_02204a10` has no scanner hit and was not tried.

## Mechanical branch inversion over every near-miss draft: no new matches (round brinv1)

Round tufold2 matched `func_ov012_021fd708` by writing `if (c <= 0) {A} else {B}` instead of `if (c > 0) {B} else {A}`. This round swept that rewrite mechanically over the best draft of every unmatched function with ≤ 40 register-masked diffs: 114 functions, taken from `perm4/best.tsv`, `audit1/best.tsv`, `splitsweep2/best12.tsv` and about 1000 rescored drafts newer than perm4. Nothing new reached 0 / 0. Tools are in `scratchpad/brinv1/t/`:

- `inv.py list|apply file func out k[,k2]` finds rewrite sites inside one function and applies one or two of them. The site kinds are: `I` (swap the arms of an if/else and negate the condition; an else-if chain gets wrapped in braces), `D` (the same, with De Morgan on `&&`/`||`), `E` (drop the `else` after an arm that returns), `R` (`if (c) return…; REST` → `if (!c) { REST } else return…`, with `break`/`continue`/`goto` too), `N` (`if (c) S` → `if (!c) {} else S`), `S` (`if (a && b) S` → nested ifs) and `T` (`c ? a : b` → `!c ? b : a`). Negation is smart (`!x` → `x`, `<` → `>=` and so on). It reproduces the tufold2 match on `tufold1/v4/ov012_0220a8d8.cpp` (site `12I`: 2 → 0).
- `sweep.py func mod base` tries every single site, then up to 150 pairs of the sites that changed the code. `summ.py` and `stats.py` summarise the logs in `brinv1/sw/`.
- **What MWCC canonicalises.** Of 1022 `N` variants, 1019 compile to the same code, and so do all 72 `S` and both `E`. About 60% of `R` and half of `T` are unchanged too. Only real if/else arm swaps (`I`, `D`) reliably change the block layout. Out of 283 `I` variants, 268 changed the code, but only three were better. None of the 1242 pairs beat the base.
- **Small gains (masked / raw), not landable.** `func_ov000_0217b324` 14/45 → 12/43 (`0I`: `if (lv <= 20) { if (lv <= 10) … } else buf[0] = 0;`). `func_ov003_021cd91c` 20/86 → 18/84 (`4I`, the `unk68 == unk69` test). `func_ov000_0216c310` 9/62 → 8/64. `func_ov000_02164c50` 11/234 → 10/234 (`1T`). The improved drafts are in `brinv1/g2/`. A greedy second sweep from them found nothing more.
- **Lesson.** The arm-order lever matters only when the remaining diff is the scheduling of one arm against the other, as in `021fd708`. For the current near-misses (register swaps, a single scratch register, pool order) inverting branches does nothing, so don't repeat this sweep without new drafts.
## Two big overlay leftovers redrafted from the target: where a `1` comes from, and per-case hit blocks (ov015, ov000, round ovbig7)

No new matches. Drafts are in the scratchpad under `ovbig7/d/`, and `ovbig7/t/v.py base func mod variants.txt` scores literal-text variants (blocks split by `====`, each `OLD\n----\nNEW` pair split by `%%%%`, an optional `#name` first line) with `sc2.py`'s raw / masked / masked-plus-stack numbers. Neither function reads TU-local data through a section base: the ov015 pool words are its own strings and `.bss` objects, and the ov000 one has only three `.bss`/main words. So the tufold lever doesn't apply to either.

- **`func_ov015_0220182c` (0xa3c) went from 262 / 40 to 6 / 6 (`ovbig7/d/182c_best.cpp`). The prologue and every case except case 10 now match.** Six changes, each from the target disassembly:
  - **Declare `bool a` inside `case 0x14`, not at function scope** (262 to 65). With `a` at the top, it is a fourth `1` vreg, and the free callee-saved register goes to it instead of the hoisted `0x1000` (`fp`). Declared in the case, the prologue is the target's: `sb = 1`, `r7 = sb`, `sl = ret = sb`, `fp = 0x1000`.
  - **`if (!(f() & 1)) a = false; else a = true;`** instead of initialising `a` (62). The initialiser copies `1` from `ret`'s register, which is live in the case. The two-arm form lets `a` coalesce with the hoisted `1` in `r7`.
  - **Load the camera pair inside each arm of the `SysB4` test** (`Ov015CamPair *cp = FLD(...)` in both the `if` and the `else` of `Ov015_CamUpdate`). This gives the target's `ldr r1, [r4, #0x24]` after the test (52). Case 0x28 needs the opposite: one `cp` loaded before the test, written out in the case (`{ Ov015CamPair *cp = ...; if (Ov015_SysB4()) ... }`, 14 to 6). Case 0x3c calls the inline.
  - **Case 0x28 reads `self->0x90` into a block local before the counter increment** (`u8 *m = FLD(u8 *, self, 0x90); data_ov015_02213328 = ...; if (m != NULL)`), as the target loads it there.
  - **Case 0x14's locals in the order `n, a, q, p, d, c, b`** (40 random orders of the seven declarations; this one gives `p`/`b` in `r5`/`r6`). After that, **`b = a & b; c = b & c; d = c & d;`** gives the target's `tst` operand order. Before the reorder, the operand order of the `&` had no effect at all.
  - **The `0x0203cb78` call takes three arguments** (`(0x1000, 0, -1)`, like every other caller in ov002/ov007). The target's `r3 = 2` is the `sys->4` store value, not a fourth argument. This changes no code.
  - **Left: case 10's constants (4 lines).** The target derives `-1` from the stored `2` (`mov r3, #2; sub r2, r3, #3`), then sets `r0`/`r1`. Ours materialises the `0` argument first and derives `-1` from it (`mov r1, #0; sub r2, r1, #1`), the shape every ov002 caller has. None of these changed it: three or four arguments, `u8`/`u16`/pointer/`NULL` types for the `0` parameter, an `s16` third parameter, `~0`/`0xffffffff`, a local `m = -1`, the `2` in a local or an inline parameter, the store through an inline, the whole `if` as an inline, a `volatile` store. About 9 minutes of `cxxperm --inlines` (1 job) from the best draft found nothing.
- **`func_ov000_02191f18` (0xb10) went from 341 / 184 to 86 masked diff lines (`ovbig7/d/1f18_best.cpp`; the old draft gives 190 by the same `md2.py` count).** The size now matches whenever the constants aren't hoisted. The old draft also didn't need its `#pragma optimization_level 3`: levels 3 and 4 give the same code.
  - **The hit cases 8–13 are written out in each case, not through `HitA`/`HitB` inlines.** The target evaluates `p[4] << 4` (and the other range arguments) at the call, after the scale computation. An inline evaluates its parameters first. It also gives each case's vector its own stack slot in source order, from `sp+0x9c` (case 2/25) down to case 13's `sp+0x48` and case 20's four locals at `0x3c..0x1c`. The `Live_02191f18` inline must stay an explicit `(h == NULL || *h != 0)` condition, or it materialises a `BOOL` (masked 137 to 77).
  - **`case 14: case 17:`, `case 15: case 18:`, `case 16: case 19:` share their bodies.** The target's jump table sends each pair to one block, and `14`/`17` tail-merge into case 9's `p += 7`.
  - **`(u16)p[0] & 0xff`** in cases 6 and 21 gives the target's `ldrh`. **`s32 mask`, with an `s32` parameter in `0x0219aae8`'s prototype,** removes the `lsl/lsr #16` after the `ldrhne/moveq` mask select.
  - **Left: constant hoisting.** Ours hoists `0` and `0x1000` (the hit calls' stack arguments) into callee-saved registers at entry. That costs one more saved register (`push {r4-sl}` against `{r3-sb}`), and the hoisted zero then turns each case's `k = FALSE` into `moveq`. The target materialises both inside each case (`mov ip, #0; stm sp, {r0, ip}`, `mov r3, #0x1000`) and only keeps `1` (`r8`) for the final `1 << op`. Replacing the six `0x1000` arguments with another constant stops that hoist, and removing the zero arguments stops the zero hoist. So the arguments trigger it, not the `cmpne #0x1000` tests. None of these changed it: each hit block as its own `static inline` taking `(m, actor, p)`, `optimization_level 1`–`3`, the pragmas `opt_common_subs`, `opt_propagation`, `opt_lifetimes`, `opt_loop_invariants`, `opt_strength_reduction`, `opt_dead_code`, `peephole`, `scheduling` and `global_optimizer` off, `optimize_for_size`, and every compiler version. The two-definition constant lever (`tdc.py`, which fixed the hoisted `0x1000` of `func_ov014_022153e0`) was not tried. Most of the 86 lines are this hoist and the scheduling it moves (the `umull`/`mla`/`adds` order of the scale product, and the `p[1..4]` load order in cases 7/23).

## Functions that read not-yet-delinked data through a section base (round datadl1)

`func_ov006_021d4118` matches as its own file, `src/ov006/ov006_021d4118.cpp` (`.text 0x021d4118..0x021d4880`, `.rodata 0x021eb6fc..0x021eb708`). The scanner and drafts are in `scratchpad/datadl1/` (`t/scan.py`, `t/scan4.py`, `t/who.py mod lo-hi`, `d/`).

- **The scanner.** `t/scan4.py` follows the tufold scanners, but it looks at pool words that point at data that is *not* in any delinks range. It reports `[r, #off]`/`add r, r, #off` accesses with `off != 0`. It clears `r0`–`r3`/`ip`/`lr` on `bl`/`blx` and on `ldm`/`pop`, and follows `mov` copies. Without those, `ov015_0220cbe8` showed up as a false hit. `t/who.py` lists every function that references a range, with its file. Only 210 ARM functions are still unmatched (outside ov004), and 82 of them read undelinked data. Just 13 make an offset access. Most of those are shared globals that dozens of TUs read: `0x02149efc`, `0x020927f8`, `0x0208eb4c`, `ov000 0x021ccd34/d50`, `ov015 0x0221332c`, `ov002 0x021f0fa0`. `__sinit_ov002_021ef1f0` needs its string table. So the lever is nearly exhausted.
- **`021d4118`.** The target loads `0x021eb6fc` and reads `+6/+8/+0xa`. Those addresses fall past the end of `data_ov006_021eb6fe` (`u8[4] = {0, 1, 3, 2}`), so the 6-byte rotation at `0x021eb702` is a separate object with no symbol, addressed from the TU's `.rodata` start. The draft read it as `data_ov006_021eb6fc[1]` and scored 26 / 4. Defining `const u16 data_ov006_021eb6fc = 0x1000`, `data_ov006_021eb6fe[4]` and `data_ov006_021eb702` (new symbol) in the file gives 0 / 0. MWCC then picks the same schedule it uses for a named object. The sizes 2, 4 and 6 already ascend, so the size sort keeps them in order. `021d1b8c` and `021d23dc` still read `021eb6fe` as `extern`.
- **`ov000_021ad130`: 21 / 6 → 9 / 0, not landable.** It is the same shape: `data_ov000_021c9fe8 + 0xa/0xc/0xe` is a separate `u16[3]` id list, not a field. The other readers (`0219d5a0`, `021a6280`, `021aa5c8`) only read the first bytes. Defining `021c9fe8`/`9feb`/`9fee` as three `u8[3]` objects and `021c9ff2` as `u16[3]` gives the section-base addressing. With the 12-byte `ids` copy written before `ids16`, the masked diff is 0 (`d/ad3_swap.cpp`). What is left is a rotation of three registers (`q`, `p` and the `kinds[i]` value) inside the `QHas` inline. Twenty source forms leave it at 9 or make it worse: declaration orders, `q` passed in or loaded at the call site, `for` instead of `while`, operand swaps, and a nested list inline (which is not inlined).
- **Lesson.** When a target reads `base + off` and `off` lands past the next known symbol, the bytes there are a separate TU-local object. Define the whole run from the section-base symbol in the function's own file, and give the hidden object a symbol.
## A derived constant from a two-definition store value: `func_ov015_0220182c` matched (round ovbig8)

- **`func_ov015_0220182c` (0xa3c) matched (6 to 0), added to `src/ov015/ov015_022015f4.cpp`, whose `.text` range now ends at `0x02202268`.** Case 10 was the last difference. The target derives the `-1` argument from the stored `2` (`mov r3, #2; sub r2, r3, #3`), and ours derived it from the `0` argument. Writing the store value as a two-definition variable, used for the `-1` as `v - 3`, gives the target's code:
  ```c
  s32 v = 2;
  if ((u32)sys & 3) { v = 2; }
  FLD(s32, sys, 4) = v;
  func_0203cb78(0x1000, 0, v - 3);
  ```
  The backend does not fold arithmetic on a two-definition variable, so `v - 3` stays a `sub` from `v`'s register, and the `if` goes away because it is in the same block chain. The second definition must come before the store. After the store it gives 2 diffs. A plain local `v` (no second definition) is folded back (6). A two-definition `-1`, `0` or `0x1000` gives 1, 2 or 6. So when the target makes a constant from another constant (`sub/add rX, rY, #n`), write it as arithmetic on a two-definition variable that holds the source constant.
